FEMALE BOAR chest 45 m RIFLE`. **FOUR THINGS THE FRAMES MOVED** (rule 5): the carry pose was the shotgun's and put the butt in the camera; the eye relief at a real scope's 9 cm put the camera inside the gun; the drawn lens filled the sight picture; and the fallback scope was a wall across it. The Director cut the first five here; **the scopeless rifle is Task 143**. **THE CIRCLE WAS THE LEFT GLOVE**, not the drawn lens: the rifle's `carry.left` was the SHOTGUN's numbers, never tuned at the version-4 migration, so on a 4.4-stud rifle the glove sat by the scope's objective and read end-on as a pale disc -- found by hiding `HandLeft` and re-capturing, after removing the wrong suspect first. **THE MAGAZINE IS THREE CHAMBERS IN THE REDUCER THIS REPO ALREADY HAS** plus `CYCLE_EJECTS = "spent"` (the shotgun's `Break` would dump two live rounds every shot) and one new `Magazine` action. **THE VIEW LEAVES THE SCOPE FOR THE CYCLE** -- two of three sources say so, and our sight picture is a black mask with the gun behind it, so staying would hide the animation Karen asked to see. **THE SOUNDS ARE PER WEAPON**, every id licensed library audio verified by name and creator and measured to load (the rifle's is 3.36 s against the shotgun's 0.92 -- the longer tail she asked for), every sample cut. **THREE DEFECTS THE LIVE TRACE FOUND:** `waitFor` searched only `row.CYCLE` so the `Magazine` step published `busy=0.00` and its window did not exist; the rifle played the SHOTGUN's shell-feed animation on every bolt cycle (three chambers plus `CYCLE_EJECTS = "spent"` leave a live chamber while the action is open, which `breakFrom` reads as "just loaded"); **the scope did not actually come down for the cycle** -- the note adopted it, the request claimed it and nothing in the code did it, now built and measured (overlay false and fov 19.9 -> 70.0 for the cycle, back after); the rifle's `cycle.shells` block is now inert but still ships; and `Viewmodel.muzzle` still applies the shotgun's barrel half-gap, so the flash origin shifts 0.18 studs between chambers. **ROUND 3, both blocking findings real and the first sharp:** round 2 moved the left hand onto the fore-end in `carry` and `aim` and left `cycle.left` at the OLD value -- bit for bit the pose that IS Karen's circle -- so the glove slid a stud up the stock on every bolt cycle, at the exact moment round 2 made the scope come down (measured after: 0.0008 studs from rest, against 1.019); four stale comments still describe the one-chamber three-step rifle (`Rifle.CONFIG`'s "open, eject, feed, close", `waitFor`'s and `runCycle`'s headers, the INDEX row's "three steps"); `magazineAtProgress`'s header describes an empty moment and a second box that it does not draw; two comments still say 0.55 against the shipped `dropStuds = 0.42`; the shell-feed case mixes `os.clock()` with the module's installed clock; the sound edges and `setMagazine`'s rules are still inside `CameraBoot`'s closure where no spec reaches them; **and `Viewmodel.muzzle` still applies the shotgun's barrel half-gap, so with `selected` walking 1->2->3 the rifle's flash steps ~0.18 studs sideways between shots -- the Reviewer puts this at the top of the queue**. `docs/design/rifle.md` still describes the one-chamber rifle and wants an Architect refresh. | **142** |

| 144 | **A respawn hands the whole loadout back** | **REVIEWER PASS for `f9215f0`; branch pushed, ready for the Director to open and merge** | Branch `task-144-respawn-loadout`, from `main` (`f3162ce`). **THE DIRECTOR'S BUG, AND IT REPRODUCES WITH A NAMED TRIGGER:** die, then die AGAIN inside `AUTO_EQUIP_DELAY_SECONDS` (0.5 s). Twelve rounds in the Forest Test, six of them double-deaths: round 4 came back `hand=[] bag=[Rifle,RifleOpen]` and round 5 was still like that -- the shotgun gone and nothing in hand, through a whole further respawn. **THE CAUSE WAS FOUND BY COUNTING TOOLS, not by reading code:** a census of the whole DataModel found only TWO of our Tools, so the shotgun was not under `game` at all while the owner still believed it had granted one. **A RESPAWN DETACHES THE OLD BACKPACK (`Parent = nil` on the Backpack) WITHOUT DESTROYING IT**, so the Tool inside keeps a non-nil Parent for ever -- and `holdsTool` was `tool.Parent ~= nil`, which reads that as "he is holding it". `watchTool`'s guard was the same test, so the record was never cleared either and the 2-second arming sweep restated the same wrong answer instead of repairing it. Nothing re-granted until the engine got round to destroying the detached Backpack, which is why task 141 saw it only in a long session. **THE FIX IS ONE PREDICATE AT THE OWNER:** `Weapon.reachable(tool, player)` -- pure, public -- is the question `Hardware.toolsOf` already asks, the Backpack the player has NOW or the character they are wearing NOW; `holdsTool` drops the record on every read that fails it. **AFTER: 12/12 respawns end `hand=[Shotgun] bag=[Rifle,RifleOpen]`.** The case was run against the old owner to prove it load-bearing (`weapon_equip.spec:544 Expected "true", got "false"` -- the re-grant that never happened), and it closes **143a's top note**: the loadout comes from `Weapons.loadoutFor(true)` by parameter, so a THIRD Tool reaches a hand in a committed spec for the first time. **NOT REPRODUCED:** the drive's own placement respawn (`Match.Body.place`), which needs two players. **PASS WITH SIX NOTES, QUEUED AS 144a, AND THE FIRST TWO MATTER.** (1) **A SHIPPED COMMENT OVERSTATES WHAT WAS MEASURED:** the census prints Tools under `game`, so it proves the shotgun was in NO LIVE CONTAINER -- a detached CHARACTER (the Tool being `EquipTool`'d when the second death landed) fits the same evidence as a detached Backpack. `Weapon.reachable`'s header, this row above and the request all name the Backpack as measured fact; "a container that is out of the tree" is what was actually seen. The fix is right either way, because it compares against the live containers. (2) the new pure case is the only one in `weapon_equip.spec` with no `pcall` round its body and no `restore`, while it parents a probe Tool into the live character -- a failing `expect` before `loose:Destroy()` would leave `SpecLooseTool` in the session, which is the state that file's header exists to prevent. (3) `refreshArming`'s delayed equip still guards with `primary.Parent == nil`, the exact question this task replaced, three lines from the replacement -- inert only because `watchTool` clears the record first. (4) `Weapon.reachable`'s header says "PURE" and it reads the DataModel; read-only is not pure. (5) nothing destroys the orphaned Tool in the detached container -- `grant`'s stray sweep walks only live containers -- so the engine's eventual destroy is what reclaims it; unchanged by this task and unreachable to the player. (6) the before/after tables should say what instant "before" is, or `r04 before FAST hand=[]` reads as if the hand was empty in the healthy rounds too. | **144** |
| 145 | **The `boar_body.spec` scatter flake** | **REVIEWER PASS for `ba0c572`; branch pushed, ready for the Director to open and merge** | Branch `task-145-boar-body-flake`, from `main` (`83daf04`). **ROW 130a, AND IT WAS NOT A FLAKE -- THE ASSERTION WAS FALSE AND PASSED BY LUCK.** The line was `expect(spread(live(group)) > before)` 120 frames after the leader is shot. `driven-hunt-runs/t145-scatter.luau` rebuilds the spec's own world and repeats only that scenario, 30 runs a pass: **as written it failed 26 of 30**. Two causes, both measured. (1) A SET MISMATCH worth ~5 studs: `before` is over FIVE boars and `after` over the four survivors, and **the dead leader was one of the two widest-apart members in 30 of 30** (`beforeAll` 22.1-23.1 against `beforeSurvivors` 16.5-18.2). (2) Fixing that is **not enough** -- the same four boars still failed 12 of 30, and over the whole six-second scatter sampled at 120/240/360 frames the mean gap ended SMALLER than it started in 14 of 27 runs, by up to 4.70 studs; nearest-neighbour and widest gap are no better. **The reason is by design:** `Brain._herdSteer` drops cohesion, alignment and the file while scattering and triples SEPARATION, but every member still carries its own `FLEE_WEIGHT` pull toward the same `field.exitZ`, so five animals running at one line converge on it. **No positional bound would be honest**, so the line is gone and the measurement is written into the spec where it stood. **The case now asserts what scattering IS:** the shot latches `SCATTER_SECONDS` exactly (measured 6.0000 against the dial) and the timer is strictly down two frames later at any frame rate (`math.max(0, scatterFor - dt)`), on top of the promotion, the `scatters` stat and the carcass leaving the sounder, which were already there. **The new assertions ran alone 30 times with 0 failures**, and the full gate ran twice green. The width is still reported through `TestKit.note`, just not a gate. **NOT DONE:** the full suite was not run 30 times (two green runs only), and the sounder itself was not changed -- making "a scattered sounder ends up looser" TRUE would be a `Brain` change and a game decision. **PASS WITH NINE NOTES, QUEUED AS 145a, AND FOUR OF THEM ARE MY OWN INACCURACIES IN SHIPPED TEXT.** (1) The comment, the request and this row all cite **`Brain._herdSteer`, which does not exist** -- the steering is `Brain:_sounderDirection`; the substance (SEPARATION x3.0, FLEE_WEIGHT 0.8) checks out, only the symbol is wrong. (2) Same for **`_step`**: the decrement is in `Runtime:_stepSounders`. (3) **The citation of tasks 139, 140 and 141 is not what this repo records** -- row 130a and `ESCALATE.md` name **task 129** (2 reds in 9 runs on identical code) plus the `831a06f` FAIL/PASS pair; the three later tasks came from the dispatch and I repeated them as if the repo held them. (4) `spread`'s header says "90 runs" where 87 completed. Also: the `TestKit.note` pair is the biased one (five boars before, four after -- cause 1 of this very task), `docs/design/drive.md` 12.8(5) still promises the spread GROWS and wants the Architect refresh, and `spread` now has no assertion consumer at all. | **145** |

| 151 | **The beater kit, part 1: the OUTFIT, both roles** | **REVIEWER PASS round 2 (gate 33/33 + 35/35 @ `e3f6e4c`); merging (Director). Notes under the PASS: queued as 151a, see `reviews/task-151/RESULT.md`** | Branch `task-151-beater-kit`, from `main` (`a1d87a5`), created by the Director. PR **#134**. **KAREN, 2026-10-10 ~22:00**, with a crop of her own first-person sleeve: *"we need same color by default what is our arms not gloves but arms green or whatever color is so all clothe is that color / then orange vest and orange hat"*, and her design doc: hunter = green jacket + green trousers + orange hat; beater = the same plus an orange vest. **DIRECTOR SCOPE CUT: the OUTFIT only; the horn is Task 152, the compass later.** **THE COLOUR WAS MEASURED OFF THE SLEEVE'S OWN ALBEDO:** the two glove maps DISAGREE -- right (74, 67, 47) over 267,953 olive pixels, left (96, 92, 65) over 265,739, generated separately in Meshy -- so the base is the median over all 533,692, **RGB(86, 80, 56)**, darker than the Director's lit sample (105, 100, 78) in exactly the way an albedo should be. It is `BodyColors` written directly, never `ApplyDescription` (which rebuilds the character's parts, and this is the module that welds plates to `UpperTorso` and anchors a tied player's root), plus `Body.strip` taking off the player's own Shirt, Pants, ShirtGraphic and layered clothing. **ROUND 1 OF THIS TASK WAS WRONG ABOUT THE CATALOG AND THE NOTE NOW SAYS SO (rule 8).** It read `PriceInRobux: 50` and `IsPublicDomain: false` and called the route "measured shut"; the measurements were right and the CONCLUSION was not -- a catalog item's price is what a PLAYER pays to OWN it on their avatar, and an experience may dress a character in any public catalog accessory at no cost to anyone (the Director's correction, 2026-10-11). Round 1's own evidence already showed it: all three items LOADED. **So the vest is "High Vis Vest Orange" (99269365249885, RP UGC) and the cap, for both roles, is "Orange Cap" (13724841516, dvdko)**, loaded through `Assets.Loader` and worn with `Humanoid:AddAccessory`. No upload, no Robux, no `assets/uploads.json` row. **THE CAP TOOK TWO GOES AND THE FAILED METHOD IS THE LESSON:** ten candidates floated as HANDLES in one row, photographed once, each read at an x coordinate I had GUESSED -- which picked a cap that renders BLACK with an orange trim on an actual head. A pixel sample at a guessed coordinate is not a measurement, and a handle in a row is not a hat on a head. Re-measured one at a time on one fixed camera with a DARK FRACTION beside the colour (the number that catches a black cap with an orange logo): the pick is (203, 97, 1), saturation 0.995, green/red 0.478 against `OUTFIT_COLOR`'s 0.439, dark fraction 0.000, against the rejected pick's 0.495 and 0.758. The vest was rendered front AND back on a green torso -- **the back is a plain orange panel**, so no Task 82 back plate is needed under it. **THE GREY-BOX HAT AND FOUR-PLATE VEST ARE KEPT AS THE FALLBACK**, per slot, never both: somebody else's mesh can be taken down, a rig with no Humanoid cannot wear one, and a source can throw -- a spec case each. `Body.accessoriesWorn`/`slotsFellBack` say on every run which route was taken. **TWO TIMING DEFECTS FOUND BY LOOKING, BOTH FIXED:** (a) the one-shot re-dress at boot re-dressed NOBODY, because the server starts before the player joins -- the gate showed `outfit.vest=cached outfit.cap=cached` beside `hat=plates` in the same run; it is a bounded poll over the characters still wearing plates now. (b) In the Forest Test a spawned player came out GREEN AND BARE-HEADED: the appearance load applies the avatar's whole look and took the freshly welded cap with it, so the window's close is a full `dress` rather than a repaint. **AND THE REAL CAP WAS INVISIBLE UNDER THE PLAYER'S OWN HAIR** -- our cap was on correctly (2.01 x 0.93 x 1.43 on `HatAttachment`) and the black was the avatar's hair, 2.08 x 2.05 x 1.94 on `HairAttachment`, a bowl twice its height. Task 77's hat never had the problem because it RAISED its crown; a catalog accessory cannot be raised. So each group names the attachments its accessory needs clear, cleared ONLY when a real accessory goes on. **ROUND 3: THE LOCAL BODY WAS NEVER HIDDEN IN FIRST PERSON, AND A SECOND WRITER IS WHY.** Karen plays in first person and the round-2 frame showed her own right arm as a tan block bottom-right and her own cap's brim across the top. MEASURED: **16 of 20 parts of the local character were DRAWN** (`LocalTransparencyModifier = 0`). THE EXPERIMENT THAT NAMES THE CAUSE: write 1 on all 18 drawable parts, wait two seconds, read back -- **only 2 were still 1**, and the writer that put them back names itself, `Players.<player>.PlayerScripts.PlayerModule.CameraModule.TransparencyController`. `Rig.acquire` ALREADY warns about it in that place's console (*"this PlayerModule's GetCameras() has no Disable method"*); it computes transparency from its OWN zoom distance rather than from where this camera put the eye, decides the player is in third person, and writes 0 every frame. `Camera.Rig` was already the one writer and already tracked accessories -- what was wrong is that it wrote ONCE, on the change, into a value something else overwrites on the next frame. Hiding is re-asserted every frame over the cached list now (~20 property sets; the 0.8 ms the header warns about was the `GetDescendants` walk, which stays off the frame path); showing is still written once, because nothing else is trying to hide the body. `Rig.reassertions()` counts it. **MEASURED AFTER: drawn 0/20 as a hunter and 0/21 as a beater, at level, 30 deg up and 45 deg down**, six frames looked at (`<runs-dir>/151-fp/`), nothing in view but the viewmodel; and in the gate, `task151 camera: 20 of 20 local part(s) still hidden after 1.5 s; re-asserted 91 frame(s)`. A consequence worth naming: `Clone()` COPIES `LocalTransparencyModifier`, so once the live character is correctly hidden from its own client, a straight copy of it is invisible in the very picture that exists to show it -- the preview script resets it on the copies. **MEASURED, DEV, `ab81e5b`:** `[harness] PASS: 33/33 (clean tree) scope=all`; client `outfit[client]: 1 role(s) counted (Alhamdulilah824=Shooters[hat=accessory])`; `task151 live: 1 of 1 teamed character(s) green, 1 kept their own head colour, 1 with no shirt/pants`. **FOREST TEST, live:** on spawn `SolidCap tag=hat`; after `/beater` `SolidCap tag=hat` + `Accessory (High Vis Vest) tag=vest`; after `/hunter` the cap alone; boot line `2 of 2 catalog accessory(ies) loaded: outfit.vest=ok(266ms) outfit.cap=ok(1052ms)`; **SMOKE PASS** (gun in hand @15s and @90s, 0 stacks, 0 error lines, 5 waves). `Player.Chatted` was measured to fire under `TextChatService`, so the design's `TextChatCommand` fallback is not built. **LOOKED AT:** `<runs-dir>/151-outfit-preview.png` (sleeve (140, 141, 92) against the beater's leg (139, 131, 89), within 10 per channel). **FOR KAREN:** the bald heads are the cost of the cap reading, and the Director has accepted them -- one word from her and the hair stays and the raised grey-box hat comes back. **FOR THE ARCHITECT, TWO DEVIATIONS from `docs/design/beater-kit.md`:** (1) that design's section 4.2 made the plates the lead on round 1's wrong conclusion; the Director's decision of 2026-10-11 makes the catalog accessories the lead and the plates the fallback. (2) **M5 and section 6 say "hair and head accessories are left alone"**, and the shipped behaviour takes the player's own hat AND hair off whenever a real catalog cap goes on (`Body.clearAttachments`) -- because the cap is 0.93 studs tall and this avatar's hair is a 2.08 x 2.05 x 1.94 bowl, so with the hair on the cap read as BLACK. It is narrow: only the attachments the group names, only when a real accessory is actually worn, and never with the plates, which Task 77 built to live with hair. The Reviewer asked for this to be named rather than silently contradicted (round 1, note). No owner moved -- `Match.Body` still writes everything, and `ServerScriptService.OutfitAssetsBoot` is the composition root that preloads through `Assets.Loader` and injects `Body.setAccessorySource`, so neither owner requires the other. **REVIEW ROUND 1 = FINDINGS, and finding 1 was right:** the appearance window -- this task's one invented mechanism -- was exercised by no test. The case named for it called `Body.strip` and `Body.paint` by hand, asserted that `strayCleared` had NOT moved, and its leak check was satisfied by zero windows ever existing; it would have passed with `dressWhenLoaded` reduced to `Body.dress`. My stated reason -- *"`dressWhenLoaded` needs a real Player"* -- was false: `player: Player` is an annotation and the only things read off it are `UserId`, `Character` and `Team.Name`, which is the stub seam the design named. It is driven now against a rig and a stub player and asserts the design's whole list (a stray Shirt removed AND `strayCleared` incremented, the player's own hat kept, a late `BodyColors` painted, the close putting back an accessory destroyed after the dress, a new Shirt surviving after the close, `isWatching` false and `appearanceWatching()` exactly **0**); `Body.setTeam` gets its own case. Measured in the gate: `task151 window: strayCleared 0 -> 1, repaints now 24, watching 0`. **ALL EIGHT NON-BLOCKING NOTES FIXED, not queued -- two were real latent defects:** the `ChildAdded` handler could destroy an accessory this module had just put on (the close dresses BEFORE `stopWatching`, and `isLayered` is true of any wrap-carrying catalog garment -- a jacket would have bitten), and `clearAttachments` read only the FIRST attachment under a handle. Also: the boot's re-dress loop remembers who it has offered one, the camera case is gated on `Config.FIRST_PERSON`, its "the count proves contention" claim is corrected (it proves the loop ran), `Rig`'s stale comment is gone, and `GAME_DESIGN.md` names `clearAttachments`. Note: `docs/research/2026-10-11-beater-kit.md`. Design: `docs/design/beater-kit.md` (Architect PASS). |
| 152 | **The beater kit, part 2: the HORN** | **REVIEWER PASS round 1 (gate 33/33 + 35/35 @ `61d9a73`); merging (Director). HORN flag OFF in DEV, ON in the Forest Test. Also fixes a GAME bug on main: a second Tool leaving unbound the player's fire/reload/aim. Notes queued as 152a (`reviews/task-152/RESULT.md`)** | Branch `task-152-beater-horn`, from `main` (`0106c5e`), created by the Director. **KAREN, 2026-10-10 ~22:10, verbatim:** *"for horn you can use hon from free library roblox / for hat and vest same / so horn will be default tool for beater"*, and ~18:20 *"we need to make tool for beater, horn, signal, something that they can walk and make sound for spook"*. **THE HALF OF HER INSTRUCTION THAT WORKS IS THE VOICE, AND THE HALF THAT DOES NOT IS THE MODEL** -- measured twice now (note 5.2, 5.4): the avatar catalog has no horn at any price and the two free Creator Store horn MODELS answer `LoadAsset` with *"User is not authorized to access Asset."*, so the geometry is BUILT FROM PARTS as the shotgun is -- six pieces, six welds, a nickel mouthpiece, three tube sections of growing bore, a flare, a bell and a leather cord, no asset and no upload. **THE VOICE LOADS AND IS SIXTY SECONDS LONG**, which the research note could not know: `rbxassetid://9038902877` is `IsLoaded = true` with **`TimeLength = 60.63`** -- a vintage recording of a horn blown over and over, not one blast. So the clip is CUT, and **the cut was measured off its own loudness profile** (`PlaybackLoudness` at render rate, peak per 0.1 s bucket): first call **0.2-4.0 s**, the clip's own silence 4.1-5.0, the **second call at 5.1**. `SOUND_START` 0.12 (past the dead lead-in, so the horn sounds ON the click), `SOUND_CUT` 4.4 (inside the silence, so the stop cannot click, and before 5.1, so one blast is one signal). **ONE NUMBER OF THE DISPATCH'S MOVED AND IT IS SAID PLAINLY: the cooldown is 5 s, not 3.** A 3 s cooldown over a 4.0 s call chops the last note AND lets a beater clicking on it produce one continuous unbroken horn, which is the exact thing a cooldown exists to prevent; 5 is the smallest value that fits a whole call. All four are DATA and the spec asserts only the relations between them. **THE SPOOK ADDS NO BOAR RULE AND NO BOAR NUMBER.** `Runtime:hearHorn(at, radiusStuds)` sweeps its own animals and calls the EXISTING `Brain:spook(at)` -- the one door every fright comes through, the same one `ForestTest`'s phantom beaters use -- with the `shot` argument left nil, so task 150's one-way shot latch does NOT fire and `_lastShotAt`/`_lastShotPos` are untouched: a beater walking a wood blowing every five seconds cannot empty it, and an animal spawned later cannot inherit a bang nobody fired. The radius is the HORN's (`SPOOK_M = 80`, `SPOOK_STUDS = 286`, both spellings written out like task 150's radii) because how far a signal carries is a property of the signal. **"AWAY FROM THE BEATER" MEANS WHAT THE DRIVE ALREADY MEANS BY IT, and that is written down rather than quietly assumed:** `Brain:_latchFlight` heads the animal for `field.exitZ` and makes the fright a SWERVE (*"the exit is the heading and the gun is a swerve ... driven game breaks PAST the guns"*, task 139). A beater pushing animals toward the hunters' line is what that rule is FOR. **MEASURED LIVE, Forest Test:** one blast, 13 animals -- **12 of 12 inside 286 studs ran** at 21.3-23.0 studs/s and **every one ended FARTHER from the beater** (+5 to +34 studs in 1.5 s). A second trace with a 1.5 s CONTROL window before the blast: of 18 inside, **10 went from under 6 studs/s to 15.9-21.0** (the other 8 were already running), and of **7 outside at 295-341 studs, 0 started running** -- each walking at 4.5-4.6 studs/s before and 4.5-4.6 after. The boundary is visible in the rows: **276 ran, 295 did not.** Console: `[ForestTest] horn blown at (3029, 144, -2095): 12 boar(s) pushed within 286 studs (80 m)`. **WHO CARRIES ONE:** `Weapon.Horn.setCarryPolicy` injected at each composition root as `Body.teamOf(player) == "Drivers"` -- the same fact the outfit is chosen from, so a beater's horn and a beater's vest can never disagree -- with a 2 s level-triggered sweep for the same measured reason the gun has one (Task 34: the drive respawns players to place them and the Tool dies with the character). Measured: a hunter spawns `team=Shooters character=[Shotgun] backpack=[Rifle]` and **no horn anywhere**; after `/beater`, `team=Drivers character=[Horn] backpack=[Rifle,Shotgun]` -- in the CHARACTER, not the Backpack. The click is `Tool.Activated` read on the SERVER: no remote, no client module, no input binding. **AND THE TASK FOUND A DEFECT BY LOOKING (rule 5), which is the one thing here that is not the horn.** The first first-person frame of a beater holding the horn was a **SHOTGUN**. `Camera.Viewmodel`'s source is *"the Handle of the Tool in the local character"* and until this task there had only ever been one kind of Tool in the game -- so it took the HORN's Handle, `weaponId()` answered nil, `Weapons.row(nil) or Shotgun.CONFIG` answered the shotgun, and it drew the wrong object out of the right Handle. `Camera.Rig` carries the same assumption in its own header (*"In first person the viewmodel IS the gun"*) and hides everything the character holds. **Karen plays in first person, so that frame was the whole of what she would have seen.** Fixed with ONE shared fact asked in two places -- `Weapons.isWeaponTool` (attribute first, name as the fallback for a Tool from a server older than task 140): `Viewmodel` draws nothing for a non-weapon Tool and `Rig` leaves a non-weapon Tool VISIBLE. They are exact complements, so the thing in the hand is on screen exactly ONCE -- never the "two shotguns at once" Rig's header records, and never nothing. **LOOKED AT:** `<runs-dir>/152-horn-fp.png` (first person: brass taper, flared bell, nickel mouthpiece, leather cord, no shotgun), `152-horn-preview.png` (third person, orange cap + vest from 151, horn in hand), `152-horn-blow.png` (the raised tilt). **NOT BUILT, AND NAMED RATHER THAN HIDDEN:** there is no first-person horn VIEWMODEL. The real Tool is drawn instead, so the horn is in view but **no hands are**, and the "raise to mouth" is a tilt of `Tool.Grip` (measured: `0,0,0.25` -> `0,0.205,0.143`) with the ARM left where it is -- raising an arm means writing the character's Motor6Ds or its Animator, a second writer on a character that has exactly one per concern (rule 3). A posed first-person horn with hands is the camera system's own task. **ROUND 2, AFTER THE DIRECTOR'S GATE RUNS, THREE THINGS.** **(a) THE HORN IS A COIL NOW** -- the first build read as *"a straight brass tube / spyglass"* and he was right; a Fuerst-Pless or Parforce horn is a WOUND tube, and the note's own source (the Oldenburger Jagdcenter signal horn) is one. Five cylinders laid as **chords between points ON a 230-degree arc** of radius 0.38, so consecutive sections share an endpoint exactly and no joint can gap however the numbers move; bore 0.09 -> 0.22, nickel cup, flare, bell, and the carrying cord closing the C. Nine parts, no asset. **Gate: `5 section(s) over a 230-degree arc of radius 0.38; total turn 184 degrees`** -- a straight tube scores **0** on that number, which is the case that fails if anybody straightens it again. **AND THE CARRY POSE WAS LOOKED AT, NOT REASONED ABOUT (rule 5):** of the Director's two offers the task picks **HOLD IT** rather than hide-until-a-blast, because hiding would make `Camera.Rig`'s rule depend on the horn's per-player blast TIMING -- the camera would read weapon state every frame and the horn would pop in and out of the hand. Four candidate grips were set on a live horn and photographed; the frames settled the SIGN of Grip's Y (positive LOWERS it, the opposite of the first guess) and the kept value (-0.80, 0.70, 0.45, roll 25) shows about a fifth of the coil in the bottom-right with the view clear. **(b) THE `[harness2]` FAILURES WERE THE OWNER'S DECISION NOT YET IN THE SPECS.** `Rig` leaves a non-weapon Tool visible ON PURPOSE -- a horn has no viewmodel, so hiding it leaves a beater holding nothing -- so `camera_client.spec`'s three cases (:634 the driver's viewmodel claim, :1658 the hidden body, :1698 ADS) exclude **exactly that Tool** by the same shared fact `Weapons.isWeaponTool` through `Rig.toolAbove`. **The body check is not weakened**: every other part must still be hidden, and the ADS case COUNTS what it skipped so "the horn is the only thing drawn" is a number. Also fixed: the `Viewmodel` guard refused a Handle with **no Tool above it at all**, which is every Handle a spec injects through `setSource` -- it refuses a non-weapon TOOL now. **(c) THE SECOND ONE-PLAYER FAILURE WAS NOT THE HORN, AND IT IS ONE CAUSE AND NOT TWO.** `Hud.renderScore` hides the score board while the report panel is open, and `ReportPanel.onDriveChanged` **auto-opens the panel once when a drive closes** -- so a live drive ending part-way through a run emptied `match_client`'s three score cases ("Expected 2, got 0") and broke `report_panel`'s *"not visible until something opens it"*, because a drive report **is** something opening it. Both were unstated preconditions and both state themselves now: the score describe closes the panel and puts it back, and the report case asks the panel's **own** `autoOpens` counter while still asserting that `Visible` agrees with `isOpen()`. **MEASURED: two consecutive one-player runs at `9a51002`, both PASS 33/33, and the condition FIRED in the second** (`report_panel: a drive report auto-opened the panel 9 time(s) before this case`) -- the fix was exercised, not dodged. **STILL UNEXPLAINED AND SAID SO: `[shooter] zz_tie_to_a_tree.spec:250`.** The shooter's unsafe shot was never judged a violation. The MECHANISM was checked rather than guessed: **nothing in the penalty path reads a Tool** -- `Body.driverPositions` reads `character.PrimaryPart`, and `Penalty.luau` and `SafetyArc.luau` contain no reference to `Tool`, `Backpack` or a weapon -- so the horn cannot change who is judged a driver or where he stands. The hypothesis is that the DRIVER client's three camera failures perturbed that run (`camera_client.spec:634` fails BEFORE `fake.aiming = false`, leaving that client aiming for the rest of its session) and that a clean driver client restores it. **That is a prediction, not a finding.** **NOT DONE BY INSTRUCTION:** the Forest Test is closed, so no smoke was run and nothing here says "ready for Karen"; the round-2 frames are from a throwaway DEV Play session. **FOR KAREN'S EYE:** the brass reads as flat yellow under the DEV plate's bright sky and as brass in the Forest Test's shade -- two lighting environments disagree, so `LOOK.brass` is not changed on the strength of one. **ROUND 3, AFTER THE DIRECTOR'S SECOND GATE PAIR (one-player PASS 33/33; `test2` 33/35, the camera cases GREEN, `zz_tie_to_a_tree:250` still failing).** **TWO OF HIS THREE HYPOTHESES ARE REFUTED BY EXPERIMENT, not by reading.** The server's own judgement was run in a live Play session with a real horn on a stub driver as the ONLY variable -- the horn built by its own owner, `Body.driverPositions` with a stub players service, `Penalty.judge` with its real arguments: `NO HORN drivers=1 firstHit=StubDriver.HumanoidRootPart @24.50 stopAt=24.50 reach=28.50 along=25.00 GUILTY=true` and `WITH HORN` **identical**. And read off the LIVE Instances rather than off the source, **all ten horn parts are `CanQuery=false CanTouch=false CanCollide=false Massless=true`** with the character's `PrimaryPart` intact. So the horn blocks no ray and shortens no `stopAt` (hypothesis 1 dead), `driverPositions` finds the driver either way and nothing of his moved (hypothesis 2 dead), and `SAFETY_GRACE_SECONDS` is **0** so the grace is not it. The round-2 guess (`camera_client:634` leaving the driver aiming) was refuted by the Director's own run: the camera passed and the tie still failed. **I do not have the mechanism and I am not asserting one.** **THE HORN IS BORN OFF NOW, WHICH IT SHOULD HAVE BEEN IN ROUND 1.** CLAUDE.md: a new feel-critical path is born dark, and all three of this one's halves are feel (how loud, how often, does a drive visibly move). **Not having the row was the miss that left the Director with no A/B but an uncommitted edit.** `Flags.DEFAULTS.HORN.default = false`, read ONCE at each composition root and passed inward; `Horn.carriesFor(on, team)` is pure so both worlds are reachable in a spec while the flag sits at one. **OFF, nobody is granted anything and the drive is bit-for-bit the game before this task -- so the next `test2` IS experiment (3): pass means the GRANT causes it and the bisect is next; fail means the horn is exonerated and the cause is elsewhere on this branch.** The Forest Test keeps it on in its own root, as it does `BOAR_MODEL`, `PACE`, `MOVE` and `LINE`, because Karen plays there to judge the thing. **AND THE TIE SPEC NOW SAYS WHY IT FAILED instead of only that it did**, with the assertion untouched: `violations=0` alone cannot tell *"no shot was fired"* from *"a shot was fired and was not judged"* -- two defects in two systems, and it is what sent this task down BOTH wrong paths. The note prints both players' positions now against where the shot was staged plus each drift, the `miss` against `SAFETY_MISS_STUDS` (12), and **the gun's barrels and reserve at the cue against now**, which change on a shot and on nothing else. **MEASURED: two consecutive one-player runs at `4c819f7`, both PASS 33/33.** **ROUND 4 -- THE HORN IS EXONERATED AND THE REAL CAUSE IS A GAME BUG ON MAIN.** The Director's isolation run (`HORN` OFF) still failed `zz_tie_to_a_tree`, and the diagnostic note added in round 3 named the half: **both barrels Spent AT THE CUE AND AT THE END**, so the tie scenario's own `R` never reloaded and **no shot was ever fired** -- nothing was judged because nothing was asked. **THE CAUSE IS `src/client/Weapon/Input.luau`, IT IS PRE-EXISTING ON MAIN, AND A PLAYER CAN REACH IT.** The four `DrivenHunt.Weapon.*` actions were unbound whenever a watched Tool left -- `tool.Unequipped:Connect(unbind)` outright, and `forgetTool` on `boundTo == tool` -- **without either asking whether the player still had a weapon in hand**. With `RIFLE` on a player carries TWO Tools and Roblox's signals are DEFERRED (that file says so twice), so on a hotbar switch the unbind can take the trigger that was just bound for the other gun: **no fire, no reload, no aim, no ammo switch until he equips again.** **MEASURED in a Play session, the four actions by name:** before a second weapon-named Tool `Aim,Fire,Reload,SelectAmmo`; equipped `Aim,Fire,Reload,SelectAmmo`; **destroyed `NONE`** with the real gun still in the character; **after the fix `Aim,Fire,Reload,SelectAmmo`.** **THE RULE IS NOW STATED ONCE AND IN BOTH PLACES: the actions belong to whatever weapon IS in the hand, and only a player holding none loses them.** Both paths need it -- `forgetTool` also runs on `Destroying` and disconnects the hooks FIRST, so the Tool's own `Unequipped` handler never gets to speak. Found by measuring three times: the `boundTo` guard alone did not clear it, nor did rebinding in `Unequipped`, until `forgetTool` got the same rule. **AND MY OWN SPEC PULLED THE TRIGGER:** the camera case added in round 1 parented a Tool literally named `"Shotgun"` into the LIVE character and destroyed it, which `watchTool` adopts BY NAME. It is `CameraSpecProbeGun` now and carries the weapon identity on the ATTRIBUTE, which is what `isWeaponTool` reads first -- the camera's question is unchanged and the probe no longer impersonates a weapon the server grants. `weapon_client.spec` carries the regression (`trigger survived a second Tool -- 4 action(s) still bound`). **MEASURED: two consecutive one-player runs at `73a012c`, both PASS 33/33.** NOT SEEN: the tie spec going green, which takes a `test2`. Fixed with a bounded 40 s wait, the idiom `weapon_client` and `match_teams` already use, and the assertion is UNCHANGED (`keyToggles == 2`, moved only inside the bound action's `Begin`). The two passing runs measure the race: **`map keys: keyToggles=2 after 4.9 s`** and **`after 4.7 s`**. **ROUND 1's FINDING 1 (`reviews/task-157/RESULT.md`) WAS RIGHT AND IS FIXED AT THE OWNER:** the only case covering `ForestTest.beaterMode` compared it against a loop that WAS its body, in a place where both answers are false -- so `return false` would have passed it, and that predicate is the ONE switch between the stocked resting wood and the 20-second wave schedule (`ForestTest.step`). `beaterMode(teams)` now takes the roster as a list of TEAM NAMES, exactly as `leash` takes the role (a spec writing `Player.Team` would be the second writer this task removed); the live call gathers the list and delegates, so there is one loop and one translator. Both answers are asserted -- `{PUSH_TEAM}` true, `{"Shooters"}`/`{}`/a team-less player false -- plus a second case for the gathering half. Five more true notes fixed at their owners (the archive filename, `BEATER.dropStuds`'s dead citation, `MatchBoot`'s HitLog comment, `PLACEMENT_SETTLE_SECONDS` inside the horn paragraph, `lastMaxDelta` ignoring the line and the beater pad, and two assertions that could not fail). **QUEUED AS 157b, from round 1's notes:** (a) **the replay-ordering race** -- the harness replays once every input spec is READY, so a case that reads a replayed counter without waiting asserts the replay's schedule (`map_ui.spec` was the only one; it waits now, and the race is still there for the next spec); (b) **what the drive aims at when stand 5 itself is the refused one** -- `postIndexFor` falls back to the index, `placementFor` hands her stand 6 and `hunterStandX()` still aims at stand 5's x until the pick window relocks, which belongs with open decision 17.3 (`GROUND_TOLERANCE_STUDS` / excluding the two `Session` tree folders from `Data.groundAt`); (c) **`docs/design/forest-drive.md` section 13.5's markers-print format is stale** -- the code's line is the better one, and the design is the ARCHITECT's file, so the Builder has not touched it. **ROUND 2's FINDING 1 WAS A NARROWING THIS TASK CAUSED:** mapping the three `ForestTest` prefixes to the `match` scope moved them OFF the fail-safe (they used to match no prefix and fall through to the whole suite), and the `match` tuple did not name `forest_stand.spec` or `forest_line.spec` -- the forest module's own specs, including round 2's `beaterMode` case -- so a `--scope auto` run over a ForestTest edit played less than the comment claimed. Both are in the `match` tuple now as well as `boar`'s (dual membership is that file's idiom), and **`studio_mcp.py selftest` proves the mapping**: one case per prefix asserting `['match']` plus `forest_stand`/`forest_line`/`forest_markers` BY NAME (measured: 15 specs each; removing one turns the predicate false). Four one-line notes fixed (`readOnlyBoars`'s "five reads", two "next frame" comments where the code is `task.defer`, the hoisted constant in `forest_line.spec`, and the "reads the live teams" case now saying what it proves). **157b gains one more:** `docs/design/forest-drive.md` section 5.2 item 4's `takeRequest` sketch is stale against `ForestTestBoot.honour` (which reads `pendingRequest` so a request typed in `Waiting` survives to the next `Running`, which is what section 5.3 wants) -- the code is the better half and the design is the ARCHITECT's file. |

The zone table is archived with its rollback (`backups/2026-10-12_task-160_points-by-hit-zone.txt`): the zone still decides whether the animal dies, is still on the record, still drawn on the report's silhouette and still printed in its ZONE column -- it stops being money, nothing else. The owner's `unknownZoneKills` became `unknownKindKills`. `PUSH = 25` became `BEATER_PUSH = 3` and changed RULE as well as number: the credit that pays is the animal's LAST one before it died (ioquake3's support rule, `g_team.h`: *'awarded for returning a flag that causes a capture to happen almost immediately'*), and a TIE on the latest credit pays everybody tied -- 2 Hz sampling gives two beaters walking one animal up identical timestamps, so 'the last one' would be decided by userId and the loser would watch it happen. **THE HORN IS A SECOND DOOR INTO THAT CREDIT**, and it is the one a 150-stud flush could never reach: `Runtime:hearHorn` now also returns the ids it pushed (the BOAR's owner answers 'did this blast reach that animal' -- a second radius test in `Match` would be two systems deciding one fact) and `Match.creditPush` turns that into credit, in BOTH worlds' composition roots. The results table already sorted by points and showed a rank (`Score.leaderboard` -> `Match.snapshot`), so ranking was one addition: `Hud.firstPlaceRank` + `Drive.CONFIG.SCORE_FIRST_BADGE` = '1st', and it answers NOBODY for a tie at the top or an all-noughts board -- `RANK_TIED_FLAG` read the other way round, because `leaderboard`'s last tiebreak is userId and a badge there would crown whoever joined first. Hunters and beaters stay in ONE table (see 160a(c)). The start card's quarry lines read the same table: `WILD BOAR, POINTS: male 10, female 6, cub 2`. **THE WHOLE TABLE WAS RESCALED, and it is scale and not taste:** `ASSIST` 10 -> 3 and `SAFETY_PENALTY` -50 -> -10. Against a 100-point head shot those were a tenth and a half; against a 10-point male they would be one animal and FIVE, so one safety violation would have ended a player's board irrecoverably. The ratios Karen approved are what moved. **Every one of the eight numbers is hers to overrule and each is one line** (160a(a)). **THE SCREENSHOT (rule 5), inspected:** `.screenshots/20261012T005515Z-task160-results-table-forest.png` -- the results table over the forest at the end of a real 300-second drive: `DRIVE 1 RESULTS`, `#  NAME  TEAM  KILLS  POINTS`, `1  Alhamdulilah824  DRIVER  0  0`, `NEXT DRIVE IN 24`. **No '1st' badge, and that is the rule working**: one row at 0 points is not a first place. The Forest Test console had 0 'Stack Begin', 0 'attempt to' and 0 'invalid argument' over the run. **ROUND 2, THE NON-BLOCKING NOTES THAT WERE CHEAP AND TRUE.** (i) `MatchBoot`'s handler bound `pushed` to the ID LIST while `ForestTestBoot`'s bound the same name to the COUNT -- two meanings for one name in the two roots the request asks to be read side by side; `MatchBoot`'s is `ids` now. (ii) `Hud.firstPlaceRank` asked its zero test of the LEADER's own points, so it disagreed with itself: a leader on 0 ahead of -10 got no badge while a leader on -10 ahead of -20 got one. The test is now asked only of the row with nobody behind it, which is the board it was always for. (iii) `stats().hornCredits` counted every id, so a beater blasting the same animal twice inside the window read as two credits when the write is idempotent; `writePushCredit` counts NEW credits and the comment is true again. (iv) `ASSIST` and `BEATER_PUSH` are both 3 in production, so every case could pass with the two keys swapped inside `Score.kill`; one new case drives a config where they differ. | 161a | Notes from task 161, for the Director to schedule | **QUEUED, not blocking; (a), (b) and (e) WANT KAREN'S WORD** | (a) **A BOT DOES NOT SHOOT.** No weapon, no aim, no safety arc, no kill, no points -- the dispatch excluded it and it is its own task. (b) **A BOT CANNOT BE ENDANGERED BY A SHOT, AND THAT IS A REAL GAP IN KAREN'S SAFETY RULE** the moment bots are visible: `Match.playerDrivers` drops a bot by its non-numeric id, so firing through a line of bot beaters is not a penalty. Whether a FAKE beater should be able to tie a real hunter to a tree is a design decision and hers. (c) **THE LEGS DO NOT MOVE.** A rig from `Players:CreateHumanoidModelFromDescription` carries no `Animate` script -- that comes from `StarterCharacterScripts`, which only a player's character gets -- so a walking bot slides upright. The fix is one `Animator:LoadAnimation` per walker with Roblox's own default R15 walk, after verifying that asset is public the way tasks 142 and 152 verified theirs; it is out of this task because a server-side animation is the vendor's own named cost (`docs/design/bots.md` section 12.2) and because one task is one task. **This is the thing the screenshot exists to judge.** (d) **NO POOL, AND THE VENDOR SUGGESTS ONE.** Roblox's "Improve performance" says *"Pool NPC models with frequent respawning"*; eight rigs once per ten-minute drive is not frequent, and a pool would be live state across the one boundary that exists to reset it. If `[bots]`'s step cost ever says otherwise, the pool goes in WITH that measurement beside it. (e) **SIX FEEL NUMBERS ARE THE DIRECTOR'S AND KAREN HAS SEEN NONE OF THEM:** `WALK_SPEED` 9 (she walks at 16, so she reaches the road ~14 s before the line does), `LANE_SPACING_STUDS` 18, `HORN.EVERY_SECONDS` 18-35, `TARGET` 4+4 (on a ten-player server there are no bots at all -- is that right?), `GOAL_PAST_LINE_STUDS` 20, and the big one: **a drive in the Forest Test now ends at about 0:29 against its 300-second clock**, because a 240-stud walk at 9 studs/s is 29 seconds (`docs/design/bots.md` section 5.2). That is her own two sentences composed and it is a FEEL number, not a bug -- the three dials are where the line starts, `WALK_SPEED`, and `END_ON_BEATERS_CROSS`. (f) **`SHOOTABLE = false` NEEDS A LOOK, NOT AN ARGUMENT** (`docs/design/bots.md` section 11.3): every bot part is `CanQuery = false` so a bot cannot stop a pellet, be credited a hit or appear in the drive report. Whether a `Humanoid` still WALKS with that set on its own parts could not be verified from a read-only session; if it does not, `Bots.CONFIG.SHOOTABLE = true` is the one-line fallback and then (b) above is live with ONE player. (g) **SimplePath is the named escape hatch** (`ahmicy/simplepath`, MIT) if `[bots]`'s `stuck` count is ever non-zero. (h) **THE DEV ARENA'S OUTER LANES SIT JUST OFF THE `DriverStart` PAD** (60 studs wide, lanes at +-36). Flat at one `groundY` there, so harmless -- stated rather than discovered. If it matters, the pad widens in the map generator, not here. (2) `ForestTest.boundary` is a SECOND writer of a character's position and clamps X,Z while KEEPING Y; (2b) the same wall meets a mid-drive respawn and clamps the road spawn's Y onto the stand circle -- (3549, 143.7, -2990), 47.8 studs inside the hill, to the stud in both runs; (3) `Lobby.CONFIG.SPAWN.OFFSET.Y` is 0. **Fixed at the owners:** `Body.onGround` is the one rule (every placement's Y is the ground measured at its own x,z plus the clearance, a missed ray retried three times, one `[place]` line per placement), the world's single ground reader is injected, `Body.floorStep` is the net at the drive's existing 4 Hz, the soft wall is made horizontal (`Body.liftOntoGround`, one-sided) and is skipped while the drive holds the player, and `Scene.build` lays the animals and torches on their own ground. **Proof: three full cycles + a respawn in each phase, 1718 samples, 0 unrequested deaths, 0 Freefall, every living sample within a stud of the ground; four screenshots. What Karen saw as broken terrain was being inside it. Note: `docs/research/2026-10-15-one-placement-rule.md`. **NEEDS the Director's `test2` for the merge** (`src/server/Match/` is in `TWO_PLAYER_PATHS`). **ROUND 1 = FINDINGS, both right and both fixed.** Finding 1: the net read the ground with a ray from the SKY, so the first solid thing OVERHEAD was the ground and a living player standing indoors or under a tree was teleported four times a second. Measured live with the world's own reader: under the log cabin it answers **160.8** against a floor of 150.0, and under a canopy **221.3** (`Session.Mesh Tree.OuterLeaves`) against terrain at 180.1 -- **a 41-stud lie**, which is a beater's whole walk. The rule now is that a downward ray can only hit what is BELOW its origin: `ForestTest.groundAt` takes a `fromY`, `Body.groundUnder` asks from the point + 6 first and falls back to the far cast ONLY on a miss (measured: a ray from inside terrain MISSES), and `Body.floorStep` asks no depth at all -- buried (`ReadVoxels`), off the world, or falling with no floor beneath. Finding 2: `isHeld` reads `heldStill` and a tie writes `tied`, so a tied hunter was still dragged off his rope; the guard asks both. Notes 1-4 and 8 fixed, 5-7 answered. **Round 2 proof: 46 s under the cabin roof and 39 s under the canopy with no move and no rescue, then three cycles (edge/road/ride, hunter/beater/hunter) with a mid-drive respawn -- 2208 samples, 2201 living, foot height +0.10 to +5.30, NONE under the ground, 0 unrequested deaths, 0 rescues, 0 console errors; gate `PASS: 33/33 @ 012f0e5 (clean tree) scope=all`.** **ROUND 2 = FINDINGS, one, and it was right.** `Body.insideTerrain` read `Region3.new(at - 2, at + 2):ExpandToGrid(4)` and took index `[1][1][1]`: that region is 4 studs wide only when the coordinate is exactly 2 (mod 4), otherwise it expands to EIGHT, and index 1 is the MINIMUM corner. Measured at (3555, -3000): span `8,8,8`, minimum `(3552, 192, -3004)` -- **the cell read was four studs away in z**, so a character standing normally could read "buried" and only `RESCUE_MOVED_STUDS` against a half-stud gap stopped a 4 Hz teleport. **Round 3 reads the containing cell** (`min = floor(at/4)*4`, `max = min+4`) **and requires the cell 4 studs above to be solid too**, with the threshold measured rather than assumed: reading the containing cell down four live columns gives **0.39-0.56 at the cell the surface crosses and 0.89-1.00 two cells down**, so `TERRAIN_SOLID_OCCUPANCY` is **0.75**, not 0.5 (round 2's own occupancy table was itself read from the wrong cells -- corrected in the note). A standing root cannot read buried by arithmetic: it sits ~3 studs above its feet, so a half-solid cell would put it 5 studs up a 4-stud cell. **Specced against HAND-PLACED terrain in DEV at four residues mod 4** (surfaces 502.00 / 503.02 / 504.02 / 505.00): standing at +3.0/+3.2/+3.6/+4.0 reads `Air` 0.00 and buried=false at all 16 points; -10/-17.5/-24 reads `Grass` 1.00 and buried=true at all 12. **Live: four 60-second stands (the lodge yard plus three on the forest slope at x%4 = 2/3/0) with exactly ONE `[place] RESCUED` line in the session -- the forced burial, put back at 236.1, where he had been standing, which is also the first live proof of the `lastGood` branch.** Notes 1, 2, 4, 5 fixed (the scene asks from the point's own Y, `fromY` is in the three published types, the net never yields in `Match:step`, the mis-titled cases merged); note 3 answered by correcting round 2's overstated claim. **DEVIATION, for the Director:** the dispatch asked for occupancy >= 0.5 and "3+ studs deep -> buried"; the measurement makes those incompatible (3 studs deep IS the surface cell, 0.39-0.56, with air above it), so the RULE is the dispatch's and the NUMBER is 0.75, and burial is asserted at 10/17.5/24. Catching 3 studs needs a different mechanism, not a threshold tweak. **Gate `PASS: 33/33 @ 94a31d6 (clean tree) scope=all`.** **I lost the per-sample log of the four stands** by stopping Play before dumping it -- the console and the marker lines survive, round 2's 2208-sample log is unaffected. |

OFF-STAND = SAFE: a hunter outside his stand circle (166's area; the tree stand later counts as part of it) cannot fire; inside, he can. Beaters are unaffected. 3. PIGLET PENALTY: already landed in 170 (points + coins) -- only check the start card says so; nothing else. 5. Foreground only."* **WHAT WAS BUILT.** One gate, on the server, in the one place a shot can come from. `Weapon.Rules` is PURE (one function per question: `beaterInLine`, `outsideStand`, `unlicensed`, plus the closed `REASON` set and one short Hud line each) and the ORDER is the owner's, the division `Limits`/`Validator`/`StateMachine` already follow. **NOTHING IS COMMITTED UNTIL EVERY GATE HAS PASSED, and that is the whole mechanism:** `StateMachine.apply` is pure and hands back a NEW state, so `onFireRequest` now computes the muzzle and the aim ray from the PRE-fire state, asks the rules, and only then calls `setState` -- a refused shot spends no shell, moves no barrel, starts no bolt, fires no `ShotFired`, casts no pellet and publishes no `SafetyViolated`. That is OpenTTD's test-then-execute (`CommandCost`); **ioquake3's `g_friendlyFire` is quoted in the note as the pattern REFUSED**, because its check is inside `G_Damage` -- the shot flies and only the damage is dropped, which is the opposite of Karen's *"they just can't shoot them"*. **HOW THE TWO RULES RELATE (the dispatch's question):** `Rules.beaterInLine` asks *"would this shot HIT a beater"* and refuses it; `Match.Penalty.judge` asks *"did a shot that WAS fired pass within `SAFETY_MISS_STUDS` of one"* and ties him to a tree. No double handling is possible, because a refused shot returns before `safetySignal:Fire` -- the penalty is only ever reached by a shot that was not aimed at anybody. **THE FACTS COME FROM THREE INJECTED PROVIDERS** (`Weapon.setTargetProvider`, `setStandProvider`, `setLicenceProvider`), wired at both composition roots to `Match.classifyTarget` and `Match.standAreaFor`, the idiom `setArmingPolicy(Match.mayCarryWeapon)` already is -- and **with no provider wired every answer is 'no restriction'**, so a world that wires nothing (and every `weapon_*.spec`) fires exactly as before. A gate defaulting to 'refuse' would make a mis-wired provider look like the rule working. **A BEATER IS A PLAYER ON THE PUSH TEAM *OR* A BOT** (`Bots.isBeaterPart`, the same `bot.to ~= nil` definition `Bots.beaters` uses), because Karen tests alone with a line of bots. The species comes off the target's OWN attribute (`Licences.SPECIES_ATTRIBUTE`, published by `Boar.Body.create`) -- the `Damageable`/`HitZone` idiom, so the weapon still knows what neither a beater nor a boar is. **THE STAND IS ONE CIRCLE ASKED TWICE:** in the Forest Test `world.standArea` is `ForestTest.standArea`, built from the LOCKED PICK and `PICK.standRadiusStuds` -- the same centre and radius task 166's soft wall uses, so where he may SHOOT and where he may WALK cannot drift; in the DEV world it is the post the drive placed him on plus `Match.CONFIG.STAND_RADIUS_STUDS` (12, task 166's measured number). nil = no circle = NO RULE, which is a beater, a player between drives, and the 15 s in which walking to a stand IS the choice. **12 SHELLS PER DRIVE PER KIND**, one source (`local DRIVE_SHELLS` -> `DRIVE_SHELLS` and both pockets of `START_RESERVE`), put back by a new SERVER-SIDE reducer primitive `Refill` (unreachable from the wire: `Validator.checkRequest` still accepts only Reload and SelectAmmo) through `Weapon.refillForDrive(player, driveNumber)` -- **level-triggered on the drive number**, called from `Match`'s own `refreshArmingFor`, so a transition, a join, a tie and a respawn all refill ONCE per drive and **dying mid-drive is not a resupply**. It was 24 of each since task 24. The Hud readout already prints `reserve[ammo]`, so the drive's remaining shells are what it shows. The rifle is untouched (9), noted for the shop. **A POOLED 12 ACROSS BOTH KINDS WAS NOT BUILT and is Karen's to ask for:** the reducer carries one pocket per type and a pool would change what `state.reserve` means to the Hud, the rifle's row and three reserve sites. **THE CLIENT IS A MIRROR, NOT A SECOND DECIDER:** it already predicts nothing (flash, smoke and bang all arrive with the server's `ShotFired`), so the gun visibly not firing needed no new client rule. What is added is the feedback -- the snapshot carries `refused`, `CameraBoot` plays ONE dry click on `Weapon.Changed` (`rbxasset://sounds/clickfast.wav`, an ENGINE asset **measured loading in this place in task 124**: 0.29 s, so nothing was picked blind and nothing can be moderated away), and the Hud prints the server's own `notice`. **ITEM 4 WAS A CHECK AND IT PASSES:** the start card already reads `WILD BOAR, POINTS: male 10, female 6, cub -10` plus *"a piglet COSTS you - leave them"*, asserted by `match_config.spec`; that case's comment -- which promised to FAIL the day a weapon-side refusal arrived -- was corrected rather than left lying, because a cub is deliberately NOT in the gate (Karen settled it the other way: a penalty). **ITEM 5 IS DATA PLUS A STUB:** `src/shared/Licences` (boar free, red deer licensed, frozen, `allows` pure), the licence PROVIDER deliberately unwired (no deer exists and a boar needs no licence, so the rule is reachable and inert), and `weapon_rules.spec` proves the refusal on a part carrying `Species = reddeer`. **Specs:** `tests/server/weapon_rules.spec.luau`, 10 cases -- the table, the three predicates (including the circle over 36 bearings and the edge being INSIDE), one short line per reason, the 12/Refill arithmetic, `needsRefill`'s idempotence, the owner's refill, `Match.classifyTarget` on the stub and `Match.standAreaFor` per live player, and the HANDLER itself: a refused shot at a beater returns `beater-in-line`, raises `stats.refused` by one, leaves `stats.shots`, both barrels, the selection and the pocket untouched -- then the SAME request with the provider removed fires. `weapon_state.spec` and `weapon_client.spec` now read `DRIVE_SHELLS` instead of the literal 24. **TOUCHES TWO_PLAYER_PATHS** (`src/server/Match/init.luau`, `src/server/MatchBoot.server.luau`), so the `[harness2]` line is the Director's. Note `docs/research/2026-10-13-the-rules.md` + INDEX. | `src/shared/Licences/init.luau` (new), `src/server/Weapon/Rules.luau` (new), `src/server/Weapon/{init,StateMachine}.luau`, `src/shared/Shotgun/init.luau`, `src/server/Match/init.luau`, `src/server/{MatchBoot,ForestTestBoot}.server.luau`, `src/server/ForestTest/init.luau`, `src/server/Bots/init.luau`, `src/server/Boar/Body.luau`, `src/client/{CameraBoot.client.luau,Camera/Config.luau,Weapon/Sound.luau}`, `tests/server/{weapon_rules,weapon_state,match_config}.spec.luau`, `tests/client/weapon_client.spec.luau`, `tools/studio_mcp.py` (scope map), `docs/research/2026-10-13-the-rules.md` + INDEX |

Nothing about Apache-2.0 blocks this use, but the licence decides what has to ship with the place (section 4(a): the copy travels with the distribution), so the note, `CREDITS.md` and `docs/design/economy.md` all say Apache-2.0 and the shipping of the notice itself is filed as a before-release item with the publish step that already strips the test code (row 2). **ON 'Vendor the library via Wally if it is on Wally': it is** -- `lm-loleris/profilestore@1.0.3`, realm `server`, so it goes under `[server-dependencies]` and a `[dependencies]` entry is refused outright. What Rojo syncs was CHECKED rather than trusted: the package ships a `ProfileStoreTest.server.luau` **Script** and a docs folder with images, and its own nested project file narrows the sync to exactly two ModuleScripts. `ServerPackages/` is git-ignored like `DevPackages/`, so the harness pins it with its own `serverpackages.sha256` and CI runs `wally install`. **ON 'fall back to an in-memory mock': the fallback is OURS and not the library's, and reading its shipped source is why.** ProfileStore does not switch to `ProfileStore.Mock` by itself -- its docs put that on the caller -- and its Studio API-access probe is a REAL `SetAsync` fired by the require. So the library is required lazily inside one function body and never in Studio, the specs get our own in-memory double rather than `ProfileStore.Mock`, and a Studio playtest cannot write a live key even with the API toggle ON. |

So `ForestTest.beaterLaneOffset` deals the indices out across the front (0, +-150, +-300 -- `STAND_SPACING`, so a lane lands square in front of a stand), `beaterStartPosition(index)` measures the ground and the trunks at the lane's own x, and `Match.botSlotsFor` asks `place(markers, "Drivers", realBeaters + lane)` per lane and takes each goal from `Match.beaterGoalFor(thatStart)` -- the SAME post `crossedLine` will use, so arriving IS crossing. Index 1 is the push line, so a real beater keeps the middle and the bots flank her: `Lane.offsetFor`'s own property, kept and moved to the only owner that can see a front. The DEV arena needed no change (`Body.placementFor` already spread drivers by index). `Lane.offsetFor`/`startFor`/`goalFor` and `LANE_SPACING_STUDS` archived in `backups/2026-10-12-bots-lane-offsets.md` (rule 7); `Bots.laneOf` is the identity and says why. **MEASURED (her place, one drive): `[lanes] 1: 3030 -> 3030, at 3030 | 2: 3180 -> 3180, at 3180 | 3: 2890 -> 2880, at 2880 | 4: 3300 -> 3330, at 3331` -- all four arrived, 451 studs apart.** **(2) THE TURN IS WHAT CHANGED, NOT THE REACH.** `Brain.clearTurnDeg` (Reynolds' obstacle avoidance, GDC 1999, as a swept fan) takes the SMALLEST candidate heading with room for the BODY -- `Workspace:Spherecast` at the animal's own half-width, the one cast that can answer "will I fit" -- own side first, both sides before it gives up, nil when boxed in (the wedge detector keeps that case). The reach is a TIME (0.9 s, 10-26 studs); the reach ALONE was already built and measured useless in task 126 (24 contacts against 24, turning UP) because the fixed 55-degree deflection fires far more often with a longer whisker. If TWO_PLAYER_PATHS are touched, stop 'NEEDS TEST2 166 <sha>' with a placeholder; else run the review in the FOREGROUND and stop 'READY TO MERGE 166 <sha>'. Smoke in the Forest Test. Foreground only."* **WHAT WAS BUILT, AND THE TWO THINGS THAT WERE MEASURED BEFORE THEY WERE CHANGED.** **(1) A LIBRARY FANFARE, AND THE CHOICE WAS A MEASUREMENT.** The Creator Store audio search (no key needed) plus `economy.roblox.com/v2/assets/<id>/details` turned up a whole set of SHORT bugle calls from **APMOfficial** -- Roblox's own licensed KPM 1000 catalogue, the same creator as the beater's "Hunting Horn" -- all `IsPublicDomain: true`; **nine candidates were measured to load in this place** with their real `TimeLength` (2.84-7.49 s). So the dispatch's fallback (pitch-shifting the beater's one note) was not needed and is recorded as refused with its reason: one timbre at three speeds is a kazoo, and it would put a melody's timing in code to avoid using an asset that already is one. **A Fuerst-Pless hunting horn is a valveless brass signal horn -- the same instrument class as a bugle** -- so a bugle call is the object and not an approximation. Both phrases are cut off their own `PlaybackLoudness` profile: **start = "Bugle Call (a)" 1842335206, 0.42-2.60 s** (six quick notes 0.45-1.85 then ONE held note 1.95-2.40 -- *"tuturutu tu tu"*), **end = "Bugle Call (c)" 1842368482, 0.32-2.00 s**, a shorter variation so a drive that ends says "over" rather than "go" (the one liberty taken with *"when ends the same"*, one line to undo). **THE VOLUME IS A MEASUREMENT TOO:** the bugle's held note peaks at **56** of 1000 against task 163's hunting-horn note at 214 and the beater's beep at 418, so `SIGNAL.VOLUME` is **3** -- the ratio rounded down. `SIGNAL.PHRASES` is keyed by the server's own word ("start"/"end"), so `Hud.Sound` looks a phrase up instead of branching, and there is **one Sound per cue** made and preloaded at module load (163a(a)'s fix, kept for its own reason). The 163 owner path is untouched: `Match.Phase` still emits one `signal` feed entry at each end. **AND A MEASUREMENT WORTH KEEPING: a Sound does not play in EDIT mode** -- the first loudness sweep read `TimePosition = 0` for all six clips -- so a loudness probe needs Play. **(2) WHAT PUT HER BACK WAS THE 6-STUD LEASH, PROVED, not guessed.** Of the three candidates the dispatch named: a character moved 20 studs north of its locked stand went to **6.000 studs in ONE Heartbeat and read 6.000 on every sample for four seconds** (Forest Test Play, stand 5). `Body.settle` fires once per placement and would have landed her at 0.00, and no re-pivot ran. 6 studs is **1.7 m**, and every step past it was written back every frame -- the rubber band she described. **THE FIX IS A WALL:** `PICK.standRadiusStuds` = **12 (3.4 m)**; `ForestTest.boundaryPoint` is PURE and can only ever return a point **ON** that circle at the player's own bearing and height, so "put back to the middle" is not expressible (asserted over a ring of 36 bearings and from 200 studs out); and `ForestTest.boundary` additionally removes the **outward** component of `AssemblyLinearVelocity`, which is what makes the edge a wall instead of a tug -- sideways and vertical motion are untouched, so he can walk round his own edge and still falls. **The edge is DRAWN**: `ForestTest.Markers`, the one writer of `Workspace.ForestMarkers`, lays a 20-segment neon ring on the ground at the same radius when a pick locks and clears every ring when the next intermission drops the picks. A real collider (Valve's playerclip, the honest soft wall) is recorded as the upgrade if the clamp still reads badly -- it would also stop a boar walking onto the stand. The HUD's arrival dead band followed the radius, 12/18 -> **24/30**, because an "arrived" ring INSIDE the circle a hunter may legally walk in would flicker exactly where he is meant to stand. **(3) ONLY AN ANIMATION CAN POSE THESE ARMS, AND THREE MEASURED FAILURES SAY WHY.** `Players:CreateHumanoidModelFromDescription(.., R15)` joins limbs with **`AnimationConstraint` plus a `BallSocketConstraint`, not `Motor6D`** (measured: `RightUpperArm/RightShoulder` is an `AnimationConstraint`, `Attachment0` in `UpperTorso`), so there is no C0/C1 to compose an offset with: a `Transform` write moved the hand **0.01 studs**, the same write every Heartbeat for 40 frames **0.12**, the rig attachment's own CFrame **0.3** for a 60-degree turn -- and with every track STOPPED one write stuck (**1.6 studs**), which is the proof that the animator wins while anything is playing. So the pose is a **`KeyframeSequence` built from two angles in data and registered with `KeyframeSequenceProvider`** -- the engine's documented way to get a usable animation id with no upload and no Animation Editor, measured working on the server in a live session -- played at **`Action`** priority (above the gait's `Core` and the tool pose's `Idle`) with **every pose above the shoulder at `Weight = 0`**, so the clip buys exactly two joints and a blowing beater keeps its walk. The **raise and the lower ARE the Play/Stop fades** (0.20 s / 0.35 s), and the HOLD is the HORN's own `BLOW_SECONDS`, handed to the bots at both composition roots (`world.horn.blowSeconds`) so the arm and the Tool's `GRIP_BLOW` tilt cannot disagree about how long a blast is. **A PLAYER BEATER GETS NO ARM RAISE, and that is an owner boundary rather than a cut corner** (the dispatch asked for it "if cheap"): his character is `Match.Body`'s and his own client's `Animate`'s, so a server-side pose on it would be a second writer. He keeps the Tool tilt he already had. **TOUCHES TWO_PLAYER_PATHS:** `tests/client/match_client.spec.luau` (the signal voice assertions had to change: the drive's voice is no longer the beater's asset), plus `src/server/MatchBoot.server.luau` and a stale comment in `src/server/Match/init.luau`. So the `[harness2]` line is the Director's. Note `docs/research/2026-10-12-fanfare-boundary-pose.md` + INDEX. | `src/shared/Horn/init.luau`, `src/client/Hud/{Sound,Objective}.luau`, `src/shared/ForestTest/init.luau`, `src/server/ForestTest/{init,Markers}.luau`, `src/server/ForestTestBoot.server.luau`, `src/server/Bots/{init,Rig}.luau`, `src/server/MatchBoot.server.luau`, `src/server/Match/init.luau` (comment), `tests/server/{zz_horn,forest_stand,forest_markers,bots_lane}.spec.luau`, `tests/client/match_client.spec.luau`, `docs/research/2026-10-12-fanfare-boundary-pose.md` + INDEX, `PLAYTEST.md` |

| 158a | **Task 158's open items: four decisions for Karen** | todo, for Karen | **(a) THE CUB RULE DOES NOT EXIST IN CODE.** Karen asked the card to say "wild boar -- male, female; NOT piglets/cubs". `Boar.CONFIG.KINDS` distinguishes male, female and cub and the drive spawns all three, but `Match.CONFIG.POINTS.KILL` scores a kill by HIT ZONE alone: nothing refuses a cub, deducts for one, or reports one differently. So the card says what is TRUE today -- "WILD BOAR: male, female, cub" and "every one of them counts - no cub rule yet" -- rather than a rule the game does not enforce. Protecting a cub is a game rule (a deduction? a tie to a tree? nothing?) and it is Karen's to choose; `match_config.spec` has the case that fails the day it changes. **(b) A HUNTER OFF HIS STAND AT 0 IS NOT PUNISHED.** The drive has no rule for it; the only penalty in the drive is the safety one. The 6-stud leash Karen has felt is `ForestTest.leash` -- the Forest Test world's own, hunters who LOCKED a stand there, opening on `Running` -- and it is untouched. A punishment for a ten-second timer would be harsh and invented, so it was left for her. **(c) HUNTER/BEATER vs SHOOTER/DRIVER.** The card speaks Karen's words (HUNTER, BEATER); the drive bar and the scoreboard still say SHOOTER and DRIVER, which are the team names. Two vocabularies on one screen is her call, not a renderer's (`Hud.roleWord` is the one place the card's words are said). **(d) TREE-STAND CHOICE is not in this task** (the dispatch says so): it needs tree stands. In the Forest Test the existing 10 s stand pick still opens at GO, so a hunter there now gets 10 s of countdown and then 10 s of pick -- two windows, worth a look when stands land. **(e) THE DRIVE REPORT EATS THE FIRST HALF OF THE CARD ON DRIVES 2 AND LATER, and it is measured.** The report panel auto-opens when a drive closes, and both centred panels -- the score board since 1.7b and now the card -- hide while it is open. Measured in DEV at 21:30 on 2026-10-11: at `ASSIGNING 0:09` the card was hidden with the beater already held, and at `0:04` it was up. So a player reads the card for about five of its ten seconds on every drive after the first. Three ways out, all Karen's or the Director's: close the report when a drive starts, let the card outrank it, or move the card off the centre. **(f) FIXED IN THIS TASK (Director decision, 2026-10-11): THE FOREST TEST COULD NOT START A SECOND DRIVE.** Karen plays the forest in the morning and would have seen drive 1 only. The diagnosis: `Markers.build` refuses the beater pad (`forest:beater-start`) because its two ground readings disagree by more than `GROUND_TOLERANCE_STUDS` (1): `ForestTest.terrainYAt` includes only `Workspace.Terrain`, while `ForestTest.groundAt` is an Exclude raycast that ignores only `OurForest`, `Foliage` and `Boars` -- **so it lands on the BEATER'S OWN HEAD**. MEASURED in the Forest Test at 21:1x on 2026-10-11, with the beater standing where the drive placed her: terrain at (3030, -1857) = 155.81, and the unfiltered ray hit `Workspace.<player>.Head` at 161.09 -- a 5.3-stud delta. The drive therefore ends drive 1 and sits in `Waiting forest:beater-start` for ever. It would have done so before task 158 too; nobody had run that world past one 300-second drive. The one-line fix is the one `ForestTest.roomToStand` already makes deliberately (*"A PLAYER IS NOT AN OBSTACLE"*): skip a hit whose model has a Humanoid, or exclude characters from `groundAt`'s filter. **The fix, and it is ONE RULE IN ONE PLACE:** `Data.characterOf(instance)` answers *"is this a person, and which one"* once, and it WALKS UP, because a hat's `Handle` is parented to an `Accessory` and a parent-only test calls a hat the ground. Three callers: `Data.groundAt` (skips a character hit and carries the ray on, bounded by `MAX_BODIES_IN_A_COLUMN` -- a skip rather than an up-front exclude because that module is shared, knows nothing about `Players`, and the rule then covers a dog or an NPC the day one exists), `ForestTest.roomToStand` and the nearest-obstacle measurement beside it. Spec: `forest_markers.spec` -> "the ground, with somebody standing on it", five cases on a fixture slab in the DEV place (no terrain under this world's coordinates there, so the slab IS the ground); the body case and the hat case fail on the old code. **MEASURED AFTER, in the Forest Test:** `[report] drive 2 opened (300s windows)` and `markers: 9/9 stands (0 refused), max ground delta 0.00, reads=3` -- the third read is the one at drive 2's start, with the beater standing on the pad that used to be refused. **157a's OTHER half stays queued, and that is a measurement, not a shrug:** the Director asked for it only if it were the same one-line exclude, and it is not -- the forest Workspace holds **TWO folders both named `Session`** (771 and 60 children, read in Edit on 2026-10-11), so `FindFirstChild("Session")` would cover one of them. The alternative the Director already named (raise `GROUND_TOLERANCE_STUDS`) is still open, and the 8/9 refusal did not reproduce in this run (`0 refused`, delta 0.00). |

The research note confirmed every source the design cited from memory: **two were wrong** (the `RaycastHitbox` URL; "first surface wins" is not on the page cited for it) and the Stokke et al. 2018 paper is real, CC BY 4.0, and independently validates `FLIGHT.body = 120` and `FLIGHT.legs = 420` (its `efd`/`mfd` give 100 and 429 studs at 80 kg). Six screenshots inspected. **Karen played it 2026-09-25: "all good"** (`PLAYTEST.md`) - head and chest kill, a leg hit escapes, the flash plus the bolt read as a hit, the marker reads, two body hits kill, and no sound was asked for. Feel values stay the design defaults, zone tint on. Merged as PR #24 |

In the Director's runs the three per-client waits now total **under 0.5 s**. The spec waited exactly **two `RenderStepped` frames**; the owner's `update()` runs `Mode.step` first, which consumes whatever mouse movement arrived that frame, and the replay now starts soon enough to land inside that `it`. **Fixed as an ordering problem, not by loosening the angle** (still `dot > 0.999`, ~2.5 deg): the camera owner now says when a request has been **rendered** (`Camera.lookAt` marks it pending, `update()` counts `stats().lookAtLanded` once the CFrame it wrote points there), and the spec re-asks and re-measures the live camera until the reading is good, bounded by 15 s, then asserts the owner's counter rose and `foreignCameraWrites` did not. `LOOK_AT_LANDED_DOT = 0.99` is **looser** than the 0.999 the spec asserts on purpose, so neither assertion restates the other. **The two-player race cannot be a regression test**, so three new one-player tests in `camera_client.spec.luau` pin the signal it rests on: the ask does not count, a rendered frame does, landed never exceeds asked, a refused request counts as neither -- and a mutation that counts the ask fails two of them in a real run (`FAIL: 26/28`). **Both runs at the code commit are 30/30 with no look-at failure.** **The 1-player run did not get slower** (91 s before, then 90/91/90/90/91/92) and **now streams**: stdout is line buffered, so the line the Director waits on before pressing F7 no longer arrives in an 8 KiB block after the run. | 63 | **M2.8b: the wood** | in review (round 2) | Branch `task-63-map-wood-trees`, from `main` (`4a0a7d5`). `[harness] PASS: 30/30` and `[harness2] PASS: 32/32 @ 1484bac` (clean tree); 377 server specs, 84 shooter and 78 driver client specs. **THE DRIVE IS THE WOOD NOW**: `rejectTree`'s corridor rejection is gone -- it made the one place the player looks the one place with no trees -- and **2,095 of 2,737 trees stand inside the corridor**. What keeps it walkable is arithmetic: `SCATTER_JITTER` 0.9 -> **0.5** guarantees 22 x 0.5 = 11 studs between neighbouring candidates, and the spec MEASURES the real minimum over five seeds at **11.30 studs**, a 6.3-stud clear gap past the widest trunk against the boar's AgentRadius = 2. **N2 (the measurement Director decision F was about): 25/25 PathStatus.Success with 2,746 trunks standing**, `reach` 12.5 s wall clock including the 6 s navmesh settle (~0.26 s per ComputeAsync) -- no density fallback needed and no row proposing one. **N3: 273 steps, 2,402 ms of step time** (terrain 1,975 ms over 256 tiles; the wood 298 ms over 4 quadrant steps; brush 30 ms), **6,264 parts** against the design's ~6,650 estimate: 2,746 trees (ceiling 3,000), 500 brush, 234 hedge. Density measured at **17.71 trees/10k studs in the dense band against 0.88 in the backdrop**. Species measured at spruce 39.6 / birch 28.2 / oak 20.5 / alder 11.7 per cent against Karen's 40/25/20/15 (the built map runs more alder because WET GROUND FORCES ALDER, which is the design's rule working). **TWO DEFECTS THE FIRST BUILD FOUND, both mine:** the species came from a bell-shaped noise field indexed into a share table, so the middle band won (230 birch to 195 spruce to 3 oak) -- it is a flat hash of the clump's block now; and every prop step rebuilt its folder, so the four quadrant steps wiped each other and the world held 964 trees while the log said 1,869. Crowns are CanCollide=false / CanQuery=true (the navmesh sees trunks, a shot still stops in the canopy); brush is neither. The two stale v2 tracks are gone (row 58a(d)); one flank track at x = 880 remains **ROUND 1 = 1 BLOCKING FINDING, and it is PROJECT_CONTEXT's named killer: one predicate answered two unrelated questions.** `Scatter.weighted` stored the key that decided WHETHER a candidate becomes a tree on the point, and `Scatter.speciesAt` used that same number to decide WHICH species it is -- so every candidate accepted where the density is 0.42 (the drive) or 0.14 (the backdrop) had a key below `SPECIES_CLUMP_STRENGTH = 0.65` and always got the clump's leader: **every clump outside the dense band was a pure monoculture**, about half the wood. **Round 2 gives each question its own number, and makes the reuse impossible rather than wrong:** `Scatter.pointKey(x, z, seed, stream)` is the point's own number, hashed from WHERE it is; `speciesAt` no longer TAKES a key, so no caller can hand it one drawn for something else; the acceptance key no longer leaves `Scatter.weighted` (a point is `{x, z}`); and `Scatter.clumpLeaderAt` is exposed because "which clump is this" is a question of its own. The hash is the one `clumpHash` already used -- **measured flat before it was used for a second question** (200,000 points land within 0.05 pp of 10 % in every decile) -- so no clump leader and **no tree** moved: the same seed rebuilds the same 2,746 trees, quadrant for quadrant, and only the species assignment differs (spruce 1082->1090, birch 542->567, oak 530->522, alder 592->567). **THE AGGREGATE MIX DOES NOT CATCH THIS BUG** -- the leader is itself drawn by share, so the marginal shares are unbiased either way, which is why round 1's share assertion passed over a broken wood. The new spec measures the share of trees that are NOT their clump's leader, PER DENSITY TIER, against the derived `(1 - strength) * (1 - sum share^2)` = 0.2503: thin 0.262 over 1,438 trees, dense 0.244 over 1,289, 0.018 apart, and every species' share asserted in each tier (thin 0.401/0.266/0.202/0.131, dense 0.406/0.281/0.205/0.109 against Karen's 0.40/0.25/0.20/0.15). **MUTATION-CHECKED with the bug itself**: scaling the species number by the density again makes the thin tier read 0.000 and fails the tier-agreement line, while the old share assertion passes in the same run. Rebuilt at seed 1: `mapgen verify` **25/25 `PathStatus.Success`**, same seed twice same digest `940540037013158d...`, `contract OK`; `[harness] PASS: 30/30 @ a2ebfca` (clean tree); four screenshots retaken and inspected; the map CLEARED again (`cellsAfter: 0`, `paletteRestored: true`, census shows Camera and Terrain only) |
