| 72 | **round 2** | in review | **The Director looked at the renders and found a real defect the Builder's report had described as good** (rule 8). The gun came out in black-and-orange camouflage with mirror-chrome barrels. **The cause was one line**: `image_array` flips the image so row 0 is the top, and `rasterise` indexed the region masks with the UV `v` coordinate directly -- so every mask was the VERTICAL MIRROR of the pixels it edited, and the barrels' near-black was painted onto wood texels and the walnut onto the barrels. **Every colour still reported dead on target**, because the median was measured through the same mirrored mask. **It was NOT the decimation** (the Director's hypothesis, and mine): with no decimation at all the damage measured 2.00x the source's edge density against 1.92x decimated. Four checks now catch the class -- the untouched source is rendered at the same four cameras every run, an edge-density ratio compares them (1.92 then, **0.37-0.88** now), each region's colour is sampled back THROUGH THE MESH by a different route than the mask that wrote it (drift over 45/255 fails), and the region map's speckle is measured. Also fixed: classification sampled ONE texel per triangle (seven now, median, then neighbour-smoothed); roughness and metallic were PAINTED into the shared maps through triangle masks (1.4x on their own with the albedo untouched -- they are material values now); masks are feathered. Barrels RGB(26,28,34) **metallic 0.30 / roughness 0.55** (round 1 had those two the wrong way round, which is a mirror). **NO DECIMATION BY DEFAULT**: Roblox allows 20,000 per mesh and this arrives at 19,325, and at 6,000 the wrist facets and the barrel/forend join grows black wedges. Selftest **45 checks**; the upside-down mask is now a mutation the selftest fails on (drift 177) |

| 76 | **The early joiner gets her own shotgun** | awaiting review | Branch `task-76-early-join-gun`, from `main` (`25da32d`). **THE DEFECT (row 75a(h), measured twice):** `WeaponBoot` starts the weapon system BEFORE the preload -- Task 74 review round 1 asked for exactly that, because a stalled `LoadAsset` must not mean a gunless player -- and the preload took **1227 ms**. Nothing rebuilt a Tool that was granted before it, so a player who spawned in the server's first second kept Task 71's parts gun for as long as they held it. In Studio Play a join takes well under a second, so **in every playtest Karen has ever run she was holding the fallback**, not her own uploaded shotgun. **THE FIX IS AT THE CLASS, in the one owner** (`src/server/Weapon/Hardware.luau`, the only writer of Tool Instances): `Hardware.upgrade(tool)` is a single path that puts the mesh on a gun already in a hand, and `Hardware.upgradeAll()` sweeps every carried Tool. **The worn model IS the state** (`Hardware.MODEL_NAME`), so idempotence is structural rather than a flag: a second arrival, the boot line and the sweep all find the `Model` child and do nothing -- two guns in one hand is not reachable. **The mesh goes on BEFORE the parts come off**, so a template that never arrives changes nothing and nobody is ever left holding an empty Handle; `removeParts` destroys exactly the pieces `Shape.pieces` names, by name, so the `Handle`, the `Muzzle` attachment every shot is measured from, the grip and the `ModelWeld` cannot be caught by it. **No weapon state is touched**: a player mid-reload or mid-aim sees the look change and nothing else. **TWO CALLERS, and the second is the net:** `WeaponBoot` upgrades once the moment the preload returns, and `Weapon`'s existing 2 s arming sweep repeats it -- because a load that timed out is late rather than lost (`Loader` adopts it) and there is no signal for that arrival (counted as `Weapon.stats().meshUpgrades`). **THE ADS VIEWMODEL FOLLOWS FROM ITS ONE SOURCE:** `Camera.Viewmodel` clones the Handle and used to rebuild only when the source part changed IDENTITY, which an in-place upgrade never does -- so the first-person gun would have kept the parts fallback while the third-person gun wore the mesh. It now watches the source's `ChildAdded`/`ChildRemoved` and rebuilds when they fire; it is told nothing about what a gun is made of. **Tested by parameter, never by a clock**: a provider that answers nil and then a template (which is what 'late' is from this module's side) upgrades a tool built before it exactly once; a provider that never answers leaves the whole parts gun; a Tool with no Handle is refused rather than erroring in a sweep; and one case runs with the LIVE provider and asserts **no carried gun in this server wears the fallback** -- the assertion row 75a(h) failed. A client spec drives the viewmodel rebuild. **`withLoader` in `assets_seam.spec` now unwires the template provider too**: the sweep asks for a template every 2 s, and the yielding cases would otherwise let it weld the spec's fake onto a live player's gun. **AND THE FIX FOUND A SECOND DEFECT, which is why this took three commits:** `069e430` PASSED one harness run and FAILED the next. `WeaponBoot` wired the template provider AFTER `preload`, and `preload` waits for a load running in another thread -- so the template is cached a frame or more before `preload` returns, and through that frame `Loader.template` answered with a mesh while `Hardware` had no way to ask for one. The harness plans its specs inside that window about half the time, and `assets_seam.spec` captured its 'live' provider at PLAN time and put that value back after every case: **nil**. A server with no provider builds the parts gun for every grant and cannot upgrade one either, silently, for the rest of the session -- including in any screenshot taken afterwards. The seam is wired **before** the network now (it costs nothing: `Loader.template` never yields and answers nil until the load lands), the spec borrows at the moment of the borrow, and it asserts the seam is still wired after every case. **The sweep's `pcall` no longer swallows its error** (`meshUpgradeFails`, warned once): 'repaired nothing' and 'erroring every pass' looked identical, which is what made this hard to read. **FOUR MUTATIONS applied, run and restored:** the upgrade removed (two cases fail, and the live note then reads `0 wearing the mesh` with the provider wired -- the defect itself); the idempotence guard removed (the 'exactly once' assertion fails); `removeParts` skipped ('Expected nil, got BarrelLeft'); the Viewmodel `stale` check removed (the client spec fails). **THE PLAYER'S PATH, MEASURED:** in the `80380a7` run the note read `1 carried gun(s), 0 wearing the mesh, live template=yes, provider answers=yes` -- the harness's own player was holding the fallback -- and the case passes because the upgrade fixes it. Three Play screenshots of that run show the mesh gun in ADS and in hand. `[harness] PASS: 30/30 @ 80380a7` (clean tree); **408 server specs** (404 before: four new), 86 client (85 before: one new). |

Every region landed: drifts 6-13 of 255 against a ceiling of 45, shine within 0.04 of 0.08. | 78 | **The harness bug: a flag added this week did not exist in Edit mode** | awaiting review | Branch `task-78-flags-require-cache`, from `main` (`3273f3e`). `[harness] PASS: 32/32 @ 4f28c0b` (clean tree) -- **32, not 30: two new checks**. **THE BUG, in the Director's own words (Task 77):** `python tools/flags.py set ORANGE_OUTFITS on` refused *"no such flag"* in Edit mode while a Play session resolved that same flag fine, and `flags` did not list it at all -- so the Director could not switch a flag on for Karen's playtest, which is the one thing `docs/design/feature-flags.md` exists to make cheap. **THE CAUSE, quoted from Roblox's own reference:** *"ModuleScripts run once and only once per Luau environment and return the exact same value for subsequent calls to require()"*, and return values are *"independent with regards to Scripts and LocalScripts, and other environments like the Command Bar"* -- the Edit-mode MCP context is one of those other environments and keeps a cache of its own, and **Rojo rewrites the SOURCE of the same ModuleScript rather than making a new one**, so the first require of a Studio session is what every later call gets back. **BOTH RENDERED DOC PAGES GAVE A FETCHER NOTHING** (asked twice); the sentences are quoted from the generated YAML those pages are built from, and the rule this repo set for exactly that (the Blender manual, `en.help.roblox.com`) was followed: say the page did not answer, and **MEASURE**. Built from scratch, in memory, in the same context `flags set` runs in: require a ModuleScript declaring **A**, rewrite its Source to declare **A and B** as Rojo does, require the SAME instance -> still **A**; require a **Clone** -> **A,B**. **THE FIX WAS ALREADY IN THIS REPO (rule 2):** `tools/mapgen.py`'s `CALL` has used `require(source:Clone())` since 2026-09-26 with the same measurement written beside it -- the flag queries had simply never got it, and the comment on `QUERY_FLAG_TABLE` asserted the OPPOSITE, that the cache was *"CORRECT rather than a hazard"*. A wrong comment beside working code is how a defect survives a review. **What ships:** one `FRESH_FLAGS` snippet shared by both Edit-mode queries that need `Flags.DEFAULTS` -- a parentless clone, required, destroyed -- which keeps them free of arbitrary Luau (still a constant with a JSON name and a boolean literal) and puts no script in any Rojo-owned container, so the harness's own *no script outside Rojo-managed paths* check cannot trip over it. **Rejected and said why**: parsing `Source` (a second, weaker implementation of what the module already says), `loadstring` (arbitrary Luau), parenting the clone (measured to work, and it is the one thing that check exists to catch). **TWO HARNESS CHECKS, about different things**: the in-memory fixture proving a clone reads the current source -- which asserts only that half, and *reports* whether the same instance went stale, so a future Roblox fix cannot block this repo -- and Studio's flag table compared against the names parsed out of `src/shared/Flags/init.luau` by a different route, which is the check that catches a reverted call site. **THREE OFFLINE SELFTEST CHECKS** for the parser (it finds a newly added flag, and does not mistake `NAME_PATTERN`/`MAX_FLAGS`/`DEFAULTS` for flags), so CI covers the half that needs no Studio. **TWO MUTATIONS applied, run and restored**: the cached require put back -> the flag-table check fails naming the missing `ORANGE_OUTFITS`, the bug itself; the fixture stopped cloning -> `fresh` reads `A` and the mechanism check fails. **AND IT WAS DONE FOR REAL, in the same stale Studio session that had refused it minutes earlier**: `set ORANGE_OUTFITS on` -> `override set: ORANGE_OUTFITS=true`, `flags` lists all three with `override on` / `effective on`, `clear` -> `cleared: DHFlag_ORANGE_OUTFITS`. Research note `docs/research/2026-09-27-require-cache.md` (rule 1) with the full audit of every `require` in `tools/`. **ROUND 2, AND THE FINDING WAS RIGHT: THE FIX HAD THE SAME SHAPE AS THE BUG.** Round 1's `FRESH_FLAGS` answered a bare `"REFUSED: ..."` string; the `set` path checked that prefix and the two `json.loads` call sites did not, so the REACHABLE case -- Studio open in Edit before Rojo has connected, which is exactly what the message names -- ended in a `JSONDecodeError` traceback where it used to print `[flags] could not read ...`, and the handler written for it became dead code (the diff had removed the only producer of `{error=...}`). Right about the thing it aimed at, wrong about the thing beside it. **Fixed in TWO places, not one:** every query built on `FRESH_FLAGS` answers a JSON object with failure as `{error=...}`, and `QUERY_SET_FLAG` answers `{set="NAME=true"}` on success so no second shape survives; and `json_answer()` is the ONE parser, which turns a bare string, truncated JSON, `null`, a Luau error text or an array into a problem the caller prints -- so a caller cannot make that mistake again whatever a future query answers. `QUERY_REQUIRE_CACHE` goes through it too. **Checked OFFLINE, in CI:** the real `run_flags` is driven against a scripted Studio for **five bad answers and two good ones**, asserting the message and the exit code (1 for `show`, 2 for `set`) rather than the absence of a crash, plus a source-shape check that no Flags query returns a bare string. **Two more mutations, applied, run and restored**: round 1's call site restored -> four cases report `raised JSONDecodeError`, the defect verbatim; round 1's bare-string refusal restored -> the shape check fails. `[harness] PASS: 32/32 @ 314337b` (clean tree), and `set`/`clear` re-proved live against the new shape. **Three non-blocking notes fixed in the same round**: the docstring's ordered check list gains the two new checks and is renumbered (CLAUDE.md makes that docstring the source of truth for every check); the parser selftest is driven by a SYNTHETIC `DEFAULTS` table instead of hardcoding `ORANGE_OUTFITS`, which would have failed CI the day that flag is legitimately retired and did not test what its label claimed; and the research note's sources carry licence and maintenance status, with the shortfall against rule 1's "3+ external" said plainly rather than padded. |

Left alone deliberately; the Director's call. **(c) NOBODY HAS ASKED WHAT ELSE IN A LONG SESSION IS STALE.** `mapgen.py stale` answers it for `ReplicatedStorage.Map` and prints a note; no tool asks it about `Shotgun`, `Drive`, `TestKit` or anything else, and reopening the place is still the only thing that clears them all. **(d) THE CLONED `Flags` STILL REQUIRES A CACHED `Shotgun`** -- `Flags` calls `Shotgun.deepFreeze`, and that require inside the clone hits the same session cache. Unmeasured and almost certainly irrelevant at a handful of calls per session. **(f) The flag-table check only BITES in a session whose cache is already stale** -- on a freshly opened Studio the cached and the fresh module agree and it passes either way. The mechanism fixture is the half that bites always. That is stated in the code and is why there are two. **(g) `docs/design/feature-flags.md`'s `flags` row still asserts what this task disproved** -- that `require` through `execute_luau` having its own module cache is *"correct here: Flags is frozen data, and check 4 has already proved Studio's copy is the disk copy byte-for-byte"*. It is a TEST fault, and the measurement is unambiguous. Six runs of `test` on the map world at `e9911c2` went PASS, FAIL, PASS, PASS, PASS, FAIL, and the failing run's own note names the cause: `shots seen: 3 ... cue at +4.5s; 3 gun(s) handed over [+2.2s backpack, +41.7s backpack, +41.8s backpack]; rises [#1 at +4.1s None/Slug res=24/24 BEFORE THE CUE, #2 at +7.3s None/Slug res=23/24, #3 at +8.3s None/None res=23/24]`. **A slug fired four tenths of a second BEFORE the spec's own cue**, from a full gun. Nothing forged was ever accepted: the exploit window came back identical before and after in every one of those runs -- ten snapshots each, all `busyFor=0.00`. **ONE CAUSE, AND IT IS THE GAME BEHAVING CORRECTLY.** `Weapon.grant` ends with `Hardware.equip` because `Shotgun.CONFIG.AUTO_EQUIP` is true (Karen: a playtest should not start by hunting for the hotbar), and `Weapon.refreshArming`'s sweep calls grant again whenever it finds a player who should be holding one -- three guns per one-player session, nine in the failing two-player one. `weapon_client`'s `parkTool` returned true the moment it found the gun in the Backpack ("already parked") **without unequipping anything**, and the owner auto-equipped it a moment later. Then the replay's first click fired it. The spec treated a one-off act as an invariant against an owner that is free to undo it. **FIXED WHERE THE ASSUMPTION IS.** (1) Parking is an invariant: a watcher unequips the gun every time the owner hands one over, until the cue. (2) This spec's window has a NEAR end -- `ownLog()` starts at the cue, so a shot before it is not counted as its own. (3) The reserve assertions are a DELTA from what the gun held at the cue, not `== 24`, which was `Shotgun.CONFIG`'s starting pocket and therefore a claim about the whole session. (4) The exploit window is thrown away if the owner handed over a gun inside it -- causal, and it does not care how long anything took; a grant resets the state and auto-equips, which is exactly what an accepted `Break` looks like, and the two failing two-player runs had `weapon granted=9 revoked=4`. **AND THE PROPERTY MOVED TO THE SERVER, WHERE IT CAN BE DETERMINISTIC**, as the dispatch asked. `Weapon.onActionRequest` and `Weapon.onFireRequest` are the same functions `Weapon.start` connects to the remotes, now reachable by parameter; `weapon_shot.spec` drives twelve forged payloads through them and requires `stats.badRequests` to rise by exactly twelve with `rateDropped` and `shots` unmoved (`weapon_shot: 12 forged request(s) at the handler -> badRequests +12, rateDropped +0, shots +0`). A legitimate grant or reload cannot raise that counter, so there is no window and no waiting. The client case stays as the half only a client can prove: that the remotes REACH that handler. **77a(a) IS RETIRED, AND ITS DIAGNOSIS WAS WRONG.** `zz_outfit_client.spec` is `outfit_client.spec` again. Inserting a file changed WHICH shot leaked into `weapon_client`'s count; it did not cause the leak. Task 80 reproduced the identical failure -- 23 slugs, three shots -- with no new file at all. **EVIDENCE: nine green runs of `test` on the map world at `40390b3`**, one of them (run b) with the exact failing shape, the gun in the Backpack at +2.2 s. **One mutation, applied, run and restored**: `onActionRequest` stops consulting the whitelist -> the server case fails (`badRequests +3` of 12, `weapon_shot.spec:158`) AND the client case fails at `expect(after.open).to.equal(before.open)` -- so the forged `Break` really does open the gun when the guard is gone, and neither half is dead. Every print in `weapon_client` that explains a failure is a `TestKit.note` now: the map world's console comes back `[TRUNCATED DUE TO LENGTH LIMIT]` and took the first diagnostic run's evidence with it. **AND THEN MY OWN NEW SERVER CASE MADE THE SAME MISTAKE, which is worth writing down.** The Director's `test2` at `99a2f5a` came back `[harness2] FAIL: 30/32` with `weapon_shot.spec:161 Expected 0, got 1` -- and the case's own note answered it: `12 forged request(s) at the handler -> badRequests +12, rateDropped +0, shots +1`. All twelve forged payloads were refused; the `shots +1` was the SHOOTER's own replayed click, because `stats` is ONE module-level table for the whole server and `after.shots - before.shots` counted every player's shots inside my window. One player never overlapped; two players did. **A global quantity measured across a wall-clock window -- the same disease, in the fix for it.** **FIXED AT THE CLASS: the seam reports its own decision.** `onActionRequest` and `onFireRequest` return nil when the request was acted on and the reason otherwise (`rate-limited`, `untracked`, the validator's own reason, `not-ready`, `empty-pocket`, `no-muzzle`, the reducer's), one at every exit. `OnServerEvent:Connect` discards the return, so the game is unchanged -- but a return value belongs to ONE call and ONE player and nobody can race it. The spec asserts (i) no forged payload came back nil, so none was acted on, and (ii) none came back `rate-limited` or `untracked`, the two exits BEFORE the validator, which is what would make the case green with the whitelist deleted. The rate limiter is no longer dodged by counting to ten and guessing: a call that says `rate-limited` is simply made again in the next window. **THE NOTE NOW SAYS WHAT IT COUNTED AND FROM WHOM**, which the first version could not: `12 forged request(s) at the handler, subject Alhamdulilah824 of roster [Alhamdulilah824(tracked)]; reasons [action Break=bad-request, ... fire #1=bad-type, ...]; server-wide counters over the same window: badRequests +12, rateDropped +0, shots +0 (other players' shots land in these)`. The counters are context and are labelled as server-wide; the per-call reasons are the assertion. **Three green `test` runs at `fd36286`**, and one mutation between the first and the second, applied, run and restored: drop the `Validator.checkRequest` consult -> the note reads `action Break=nil` (the forged Break ACCEPTED) with every later payload `not-ready` because that Break left the gun broken open, `weapon_shot.spec:202` fails, and the client half fails again at `expect(after.open).to.equal(before.open)`. Both halves bite, and the note names the exploit rather than a number. |

Section 17 step 5's measurement B is a reopen, and the Director will not reopen tonight because it drops Rojo and the MCP link. **NEEDS KAREN at her 10:00 session: after opening the place, the Director runs `python tools/mapgen.py contract` and compares the marker digest with that string.** Until then a Studio restart may lose the map, and `mapgen.py build --seed 1` rebuilds it byte-identically. **ROUND 1's VERDICT: one blocking finding, and it was right.** Selftest 9c's `"the server says it has no boar"` fixture was two adjacent Python literals whose seam left a stray `""`, so it was malformed JSON: `json.loads` raised and the case passed through the same *"did not answer with JSON"* branch as the bare-string case beside it. `{error}` is the one shape `QUERY_STAGE_TARGET` actually produces on its most likely real failure -- the drive released nothing -- and nothing tested it, while REQUEST.md claim 8 said it was covered. **Fixed twice over, because the Director's own mutation showed the first fix was not enough:** the fixture is built with `json.dumps` (valid by construction, not by proofreading), and each case now names both the words the log must carry AND the branches it must NOT take. That second half is the point -- breaking `json_answer`'s `data.get("error")` branch made the `{error}` object arrive as data with no position, and `carries no usable position: {'error': ...}` still repeated every word the case was looking for, so "the error text is in the log" passed for the wrong reason a second time. **Two mutations, applied, run and restored:** break that branch -> three cases fail, naming `also took ['carries no usable position']`; restore round 1's seam -> two fail, printing the stray `""` verbatim. **(k) FOUR ROUND-1 NOTES FIXED IN THE SAME ROUND rather than queued.** `test_arena.spec`'s dormant note said *"its 12 cases are dormant"* and there were **ten** -- a hand-kept number, so it names the file instead of a count and cannot go stale. `studio_mcp.py`'s docstring still said `selftest` *"exercises the pure helpers only"*, which 9b and 9c have not been true of since Task 78 (CLAUDE.md makes that docstring the source of truth for the test system, so this is a documentation defect, the same shape as Task 78's). The `UNREADABLE` guard scanned for the literal `Workspace.<Name>`, so `local w = Workspace; w.StreamingTargetRadius` slipped past -- it scans for `.<Name>` now. `Boar.CONFIG.spawnPoint`'s comment described the Task 17 arena (*"210 studs from exitZ ... clear of PillarMid and WallWest"*): with `exitZ = -820` it is 840 studs and neither part is built, so the comment now says what is still true and that the value is unused in production. **(l) NO `SpawnLocation` EXISTS IN THE MAP WORLD, and nothing asserts one** (Reviewer, round 1). `ArenaBoot` no longer builds `TestArena.ArenaSpawn` -- the one spawn since the place's default was archived to `ServerStorage.Archive` -- and `src/serverstorage/MapGen/` creates none, so a joining player gets Roblox's origin fallback until the drive places them on a post. **Not broken**: the live run placed, shot and scored. But `docs/design/map-generator.md` section 17 never named it, and where a player stands before the drive claims them is a design decision with an owner (`Match.Body` places; the map builds geometry), not a literal to pick here. **(m) `stats.firstReleaseAfterSeconds` IS FIRST-WRITER-WINS FOR THE WHOLE SERVER PROCESS** (Reviewer, round 1). Several specs require the production `Match` singleton and run before `zz_drive_boundary`, so a future spec that drove it to a release first would silently claim the number claim 7 rests on. Empirically it did not: 40.3 s and 40.5 s over two runs, both exactly `INTERMISSION_SECONDS` 20 + `FIRST_RELEASE_SECONDS` 20. Worth a per-drive reset or a spec-name stamp beside the number. **(n) THERE IS NO PLAY-MODE CAPTURE OF THE SWITCHED STATE** (Reviewer, round 1). The nine images are Edit-mode stills of the built map from `d133bba` and were looked at in detail (79a(a)-(f)), which is a real look at the world a player now stands in -- but nothing shows the arena absent with a character in the map. A Play capture needs a Play session outside the harness's own, so it is queued rather than improvised. **(o) TASK 79'S FINAL GATE FAILED ON A TASK 80 SPEC, AND 80a(a) IS WHY.** `[harness2] FAIL: 30/32 @ b83d9fc`, `weapon_client.spec:967 Expected "false", got "true"` -- `after.open` in the exploit window, the same shape as the failures Task 80 was dispatched for, on the branch that now CONTAINS Task 80's fix. **It is not a forged request, and that is proved in the same session on the same server:** `weapon_shot: 12 forged request(s) at the handler, subject Player1 ...; reasons [action Break=bad-request, ...]; badRequests +12, rateDropped +0, shots +0`. **What moved the gun was a legitimate reload**, and the window's own note names it: `last weapon input 1.51 s before it opened` and then ONE change at +0.47 s inside it -- `Spent/Spent -> Empty/Empty, open=true, busyFor=0.50`, which is the BREAK step (`RELOAD_BREAK` 0.50) and nothing else produces it. That lands **1.98 s** after the last input the spec could see, against `RELOAD_TOTAL = 2.0`: an input just outside a flat 1.5 s quiet period is still publishing state changes half a second into the window, and `stillBusy()` cannot see it because it reads the newest snapshot's `busyFor` and at window-open time the gun WAS idle. **Fixed by taking the number from the gun instead of guessing it:** `QUIET = Shotgun.CONFIG.RELOAD_TOTAL + 1`, one source, and it moves if Karen changes the feel. 80a(a) STAYS OPEN -- this is still a time-based guard; what it guards is a smoke test whose worst failure is a retry, because the property itself is proved on the server. Carried on the 79 branch because 79 cannot be reviewed with a red gate and the file is already in 79's diff via #74. |

The 10-second countdown and the red rectangle she described in the same breath were **withdrawn by her the same day** (*"remove rectangle for now"*, *"and countdown"*) and are NOT built. **A SCROLLING STRIP, NOT A DIAL**, and the choice is the requirement: a dial says where north is, a strip says what is in FRONT of you, because the thing at its centre is the thing the middle of the screen points at -- so the drive's marker reads as "turn left / turn right / it is in front of you" with no arithmetic, which is the whole of her sentence. `src/client/Hud/Compass.luau` is the one writer inside the `Compass` frame the Hud builds, the same shape as `ReportPanel`. Eight cardinals, minor ticks every 15 deg, a centre hairline, and an orange chevron with the word DRIVE. **THE WORLD STATES THE BEARING AND THE HUD DRAWS IT.** `ForestTest.DRIVE_BEARING_DEG = 0` is DERIVED in that world's own data from `DRIVERS.startNorthStuds` (the beaters begin 700 studs north of the road and push south), so the HUD never works a direction out of a position. A world that has not said gets a plain compass and **no marker** rather than a marker pointing at a guess; `Compass.setBearing` is the one line a later Match drive needs. **A marker behind the player is PINNED to the near end and dimmed** rather than hidden, so "it is behind me and to the left" still has an answer. `tests/client/compass.spec.luau` (new, registered in the `hud` scope) covers the bearing convention -- north is +Z, east is +X, clockwise -- the shortest-way-round wrap that stops a marker 10 deg west of north reading as 350 deg east, the pixel mapping, the pinning, and that the marks really move on screen. **THE FRAME FOUND A REAL DEFECT** (rule 5): the first capture showed a bare chevron with no word, because the DRIVE label was positioned two pixels BELOW the strip and the strip sets `ClipsDescendants` -- so "DRIVE" was clipped away on every frame. It is inside the strip now, and the second frame shows it. **HONESTLY, ABOUT THE SECOND FRAME:** the camera is the Camera system's and reasserts anything a script writes, the hunter is leashed to his stand, and in first person the yaw is the player's mouse -- so **I could not turn the view from a script** (three ways tried: writing `Camera.CFrame`, turning the `HumanoidRootPart`, and real mouse input through the harness). The facing-AWAY frame is a real capture at heading 180 with the drive pinned left and dimmed; the facing-TOWARD frame was taken by temporarily rotating `DRIVE_BEARING_DEG` to 180 so the marker centred, and the value was restored to 0 and verified before committing. Both pictures are of the real strip in the real place; one of them had the DATA rotated rather than the camera, and that is said here because it is not the same thing. **ROUND 2 (Director: the compass is 180 degrees off).** **HE WAS RIGHT, AND MY ROUND-1 PROOF HID IT.** Rotating `DRIVE_BEARING_DEG` to 180 to photograph a centred marker tested nothing; it made a wrong compass look right. **MEASURED AT SPAWN, STABLE FOR OVER A HUNDRED SECONDS:** the character's `HumanoidRootPart.CFrame.LookVector` is `(0.00, 0.00, 1.00)` -> **0 deg, north, at the drive**, while `workspace.CurrentCamera.CFrame.LookVector` is `(0.00, -0.14, -0.99)` -> **180 deg, south**. The camera points the OPPOSITE WAY to the body, and the compass was reporting it faithfully. **THE ARITHMETIC WAS NEVER WRONG** -- `atan2(x, z)` maps +Z to 0 and +X to 90, which the spec asserted and still asserts against real look vectors -- **the SOURCE was**. `Compass.headingFrom(camera, character)` takes the heading from the BODY whenever the camera sits within `EYE_STUDS` (3) of the head, which is first person, where the view and the body are the same thing by construction; third person still uses the camera, because there it is deliberately not the body's facing. The camera itself belongs to `src/client/Camera/` -- a second writer of `CurrentCamera` is the open item from task 123 -- so the spec RECORDS the camera/body disagreement as a harness note rather than asserting it, which would fail the gate for somebody else's defect. **ALSO FOUND, AND IT INVALIDATED TWO MEASUREMENTS BEFORE I CAUGHT IT:** a Play session begun before Rojo had pushed an edit runs the OLD code, and reading a module's `Source` from the Edit datamodel answers 0 characters, so that is not a way to check. The check that works is to require the module in PLAY and read a value only the new code has (`CONFIG.EYE_STUDS`). **AFTER: body 0 deg, strip reads NW / N / NE with N dead centre and the DRIVE marker at x=0, alpha 0.00** (was S centred with DRIVE pinned at x=-180). Frame `task125-compass-spawn.png`, nothing rotated: NW and NE either side, the orange DRIVE chevron centred, the birch edge ahead and a boar out on the grass. **Standing rule A re-run on the fixed build**: tool in the CHARACTER at 15 s and 128 s, zero faults in either console, 5 waves released. **ROUND 3 (Director: the compass does not turn).** **RIGHT AGAIN, AND MY ROUND-2 FIX WAS THE SECOND WRONG SOURCE.** He turned the view with real `mouse_event` moves: the picture turned and the strip did not move a pixel, because **in first person the camera system turns the VIEW and not the BODY** -- so a compass driven by `HumanoidRootPart` is nailed to the spot. Three sources, two of them wrong, and both wrong ones look right: (1) `workspace.CurrentCamera.CFrame` -- `Camera.Rig` COUNTS AND WARNS ABOUT foreign writes to that property and has since task 123, so the engine's copy is not reliably ours; (2) the character's body -- never turns in first person. **THE OWNER OF THE VIEW NOW PUBLISHES THE VIEW**: `Rig.appliedCFrame()` returns the CFrame `Rig.apply` last wrote -- what the camera system INTENDS, which is what is rendered -- re-exported as `Camera.viewCFrame()`, and the Hud reads that and nothing else. **`CurrentCamera` IS NEVER PASSED IN**, which matters: keeping it as a fallback brought the bug straight back, because for the first frames of a session `viewCFrame()` is nil and the fallback ran. With nothing trustworthy to read the strip holds its last heading -- a compass that lags a few frames at spawn is not a compass that lies. **PROVEN WITH REAL INPUT, NOTHING ROTATED IN DATA**: before, heading **180.0** (S centred, DRIVE pinned at x=-180, dimmed 0.45); after 40 x `mouse_event` MOVE dx=25, heading **33.5** (NE near centre, N at -100, E at 170, DRIVE at -100 SOLID); after 20 x dx=-25 back, heading **298.0** (NW/W, DRIVE pinned right at +180, dimmed). Frames `task125-compass-before-turn.png` (open grass, strip SE/S/SW) and `task125-compass-after-turn.png` (dense birch woodland, strip NW/DRIVE/NE) -- a different view and a different strip. **Also grounded on a real object**: the nearest boar's true bearing against the strip's own heading agreed with whether the engine put it on screen, in both states. `CONFIG.EYE_STUDS` is gone (no reader left) and the spec asserts the new source, with the other two recorded as harness notes because they belong to other owners. **Standing rule A re-run on the fixed build**: tool in the CHARACTER at 15 s and 128 s, zero faults in either console, 5 waves released. **ROUND 4 (Director: it turns, but starts 180 degrees wrong).** **RIGHT, AND MY OWN PROOF SAID SO** -- round 3 pasted "BEFORE strip heading=180.0 ... S@0" at a spawn that faces NORTH, and I read it as agreement instead of as the bug. **SETTLED OBJECTIVELY INSTEAD OF BY ARGUMENT**: the spawn frames were compared pixel by pixel against the Director's named reference `20261005T000513Z-ft-facing-north.png` (task 122 measured that spawn as look (0,0,1) = north) -- RMS **36.5 and 36.7**, against **55.0** for a deliberately turned frame. The spawn view IS north while the published CFrame's LookVector read 180. **THE CAUSE IS TWO ZEROS, AND IT IS WRITTEN DOWN IN THE CAMERA'S OWN FILE**: `Mode.cameraCFrame` builds the view from `CFrame.fromEulerAnglesYXZ(pitch, yaw, 0)`, whose LookVector `Mode`'s own comment writes as `(-sin(yaw)cos(pitch), sin(pitch), -cos(yaw)cos(pitch))` -- so **camera yaw 0 looks down -Z** while a compass bearing of 0 looks down +Z. Half a turn apart, exactly as measured. **THE SIGN WAS ALREADY RIGHT** and is untouched: 40 real mouse moves right took the bearing 180 -> 33.5, which is +213 deg clockwise. **FIXED WHERE THE HEADING IS PUBLISHED**, not in the Hud: `Camera.viewHeadingDeg()` converts this system's own convention to a world bearing in one expression, in the one file that should know both; the compass reads a bearing and does no arithmetic on it. `compass.spec` now drives the conversion with the CAMERA's real yaw values -- its inverse (`atan2(-x,-z)`) and its forward (`fromEulerAnglesYXZ`) -- for all four cardinals, which is the assertion that would have caught this in round 1 and did not exist. **PROVEN AGAINST REALITY**: at spawn **heading 0.0, N@0 dead centre, DRIVE x=0 SOLID**, and that frame is RMS **37.1** from the north reference (the same 36-37 band as the known north frames); after a real ~90 deg right turn, **heading 107.0, E near centre (E@-51), DRIVE pinned left and dimmed**; turning back, 325.2 (NW/W). | 127 | **One side to the other, in a line** | **IN THE STACK PR 122-130; GATE GREEN AT THE HEAD; THE LINE FORMS, TWO TARGETS NOT MET AND SAID PLAINLY** | Branch `task-127-boar-line`, from `task-125-stand-hud` (`e282669`). Karen, 2026-10-05: *"they not in one line crossing all is spread"*, *"imagine they come from one side in one line and crossing road thats it"*, *"it's simple"*. **THE LINE IS A TRAIL, NOT A FORMATION, and that is the whole design.** Task 126 built a file by having each follower STEER at a point behind the animal ahead and measured the result: the gap stayed at 23-35 studs and the turning went UP, because steering at a moving point is a chase and a chase wanders. Here the leader walks a `PathfindingService` route computed ONCE (agent radius 2.5, no jumping, trunks are `CanCollide` obstacles) and **drops a breadcrumb every stud**; follower k stands at `k x SPACING` measured **along that trail** -- arc length, not straight-line distance -- so it walks exactly where she walked. `src/server/ForestTest/Line.luau` is that arithmetic, pure and spec-able; `Brain:leadTo(point, speed)` is the one new door, the same shape as `urge`, `spook` and `check`, and in that mode every blend is OFF: no whiskers, no route of its own, no sounder cohesion, no flee vector. **MEASURED, 160 s traces, hunter locked at a stand.** **WHAT WORKS:** the followers really do ride her path -- **offset from the leader's own traced path, median 1.3-2.0 studs** (the Director's target was <= 1.5), against the 18-30 studs of "spread" the same measurement gave in task 126; the pace mix comes out walk/trot/run as asked; the crossing point varies from **-79 to +105 studs** of her stand; **nobody goes back to the drive side** (0 of ~40 in the best run, 2 in the worst); and after a shot **17-30 animals move and 0-4 of them northward** -- they run forward over the road and away. Seven lines released in 165 s, two of them double lines. **WHAT IS NOT MET, AND IT IS TWO OF THE SEVEN:** the nose-to-nose **gap is loose -- median 12.7-14.1 against a station spacing of 8.5**, with a p90 of 142-213 where a line has broken; and **trunk contacts (16-41 distinct pairs) and stuck animals (23-36) are not zero**. The frames agree with the numbers and are described honestly: four boars crossing together on one axis -- much tighter than the old spread, but a loose string rather than a tidy file. **THREE BUGS FOUND BY MEASUREMENT, EACH WITH ITS NUMBER.** (1) **Every route failed** -- `placed=9 routes made=0` on every wave -- because the boars were spawned ON the start point first and `ComputeAsync` from inside nine `CanCollide` bodies fails; the routes are computed BEFORE anything is spawned now, which also means a wave whose route cannot be found never leaves loose animals behind. (2) **The spacing was physically impossible**: 3 studs centre-to-centre on a 5.5-stud body, so eight animals overlapped by 2.5 studs each and the solver spent every frame shoving them apart -- 9.9-stud median gaps, 29 contacts, 23 stuck. It is 8.5 centre to centre now (body + the Director's 3-stud gap). (3) **The route ended 20 studs NORTH of the despawn line**, so every line walked its whole route, arrived and stood there for ever -- visible as a cluster of animals going still at 134-152 s, which is exactly how long the route takes. **ROUND 2 (Director: stuck and contacts to zero, and a REAL line).** Every number moved, and the line stopped BREAKING -- which was the biggest of them. **BEFORE -> AFTER, same trace, same scoring:** gap median **14.1 -> 10.2** (station 8.5); gap **p90 213.1 -> 26.2**; gap **max 450 -> 77**; **breaks (> 3 x spacing) 26.7% of pairs -> 10.5%**; trunk contacts **16-41 distinct pairs over 90-106 ticks -> 12 over 56**; stuck **23-36 -> 18**; offset from the leader's own path median **1.3-2.0 -> 1.9**; back north after crossing **0**. **THREE CAUSES, FOUND FROM THE TRACE.** (1) **A follower was ordered STRAIGHT AT its station**, so when the station was round a corner it was being ordered to CUT that corner -- through whatever the leader had gone round, which is a trunk. `Line.arcAt` now tells each follower where it is ON the trail and it is ordered to a point `lookaheadStuds` (4) FURTHER ALONG the same polyline, so the thing it walks is the leader's own path and never a chord across it. (2) **The follower's speed was driven by a EUCLIDEAN distance**, which cannot tell "behind" from "overshot" -- both read positive, so both sped up. It is a SIGNED arc-length error now, with the Director's +-40 %. (3) **The sow never looked back.** `Line.leaderSpeed` eases her toward `tailSlow` (0.45) of her pace once the LAST animal is more than `tailPatienceStuds` (12) behind its station -- which is what a real sow does and what took the p90 from 213 studs to 26. **AND THE ROUTE IS NOW SWEPT WITH THE ANIMAL'S OWN BODY** (the Director's item 1a): `PathfindingService` guarantees the WAYPOINTS are reachable for an agent of a given radius, not that the straight line between two of them is clear, and `WaypointSpacing` is 8 studs -- so `ForestTest.clearRoute` `Blockcast`s a 3 x 3 x 1 box along every segment and inserts a sidestep where it clips. A wedged FOLLOWER is also put back on the trail behind the one ahead (`Runtime:placeAt`), which is the one place certainly clear; it is a last resort and its comment says so, because a setpoint cannot free a body the solver is holding. **ONE OF MY OWN METRICS WAS WRONG AND IS FIXED**: the stuck count scored an animal standing still as stuck, and this round's "sow waits for her tail" makes animals stand still ON PURPOSE -- so it now requires the ORDERED speed to have been above 2 for the whole window, published on the part because a sandboxed `require` cannot see it. **STILL NOT ZERO, AND THE FRAME AGREES:** 12 contact pairs and 18 stuck against a target of 0, a 10.2 median gap against a station of 8.5 (target +-1.5), and `task127-line-nose-to-tail.png` shows six animals in a loose diagonal band at 50-105 studs with two of them SIDE BY SIDE -- much tighter than the old scatter and all travelling one way, but **it does not read as a nose-to-tail line**. **Standing rule A re-run, 97 s Play**: tool in the CHARACTER at 15 s and 97 s, zero faults in either console, 4 lines released, **worst simultaneous playing sounds = 3**. **Standing rule A, fresh 165 s Play**: tool in the CHARACTER at 15 s and 165 s, never in the Backpack; **zero faults in either console**; 7 lines released; **worst simultaneous playing sounds = 3** (the Director's ceiling). Frames `task127-line-crossing.png` and `task127-line-flight.png` inspected. **The old wave behaviour is kept and switchable**: `ForestTest.LINE.enabled = false` restores the pushes, turn-backs, lateral passes and phantom beaters untouched. **NOT DONE:** the harness gate (Studio has the Forest Test place open, not DEV) and no review round |
 **ROUND 3 (the Director's hypothesis, tested and REFUTED, then the real bug found).** He asked first whether boar-to-boar COLLISION was behind the loose gaps, the side-by-side pairs and the stuck animals. `Boar.CONFIG.BOAR_COLLISION` puts every body in a collision group that does not collide with itself (terrain, trunks, players and `CanQuery` all unchanged), and it was verified LIVE as applied -- `CollisionGroupsAreCollidable("DrivenHuntBoars", "DrivenHuntBoars")` false, against `Default` true, and bodies seen overlapping. **It changed nothing:** pairs abreast 36 % -> 34 %, gap median 10.2 -> 10.6, p90 26.2 -> 34.2, stuck 18 -> 19, distinct trunk contacts 12 -> 21. So it ships OFF, kept with its table in the comment so nobody spends another round on it. **THE CONTACT SPLIT the Director asked for: the LEADER is the bigger half** (11-14 of 16-21 distinct pairs). Widening her route's agent radius 2.5 -> 4 did not move it either (16 contacts, 11 of them leaders), and then the navmesh itself was asked in the Edit place: a path computed straight through a trunk of radius 0.9 came back Success with its nearest waypoint 3.9 studs from the trunk centre -- **the mesh does see the trunks and does hold the clearance**, so a leader who touches one is not on her route at that moment (spooked, running straight, which is what Karen asked for). Radius reverted to 2.5 with that written down. **THE ONE REAL FIX, and it was in my own code:** a follower's aim point was `min(ownS + lookahead, stationS)`, so a follower sitting within a lookahead of its station was handed a point UNDER ITS OWN FEET, inside `ARRIVE_STUDS`; the Brain reported it had arrived and stood there while the line walked on. That is what the stuck count had been counting all along -- told to move, not moving, and nothing in front of it. The aim is now always a lookahead further along the trail and the station is regulated by SPEED alone, plus a follower that has overrun now WAITS (speed 0) instead of being walked backwards. **Stuck 18 (round 2) -> 33 (with the wait alone, which exposed the bug) -> 12.** Gaps did not move (median 10.1, p90 34.5) and pairs abreast did not (40 %), and I say that plainly: the file still does not read nose to tail from the stand -- I looked at four frames and in the best of them the animals are a loose row at one depth, not a line. `tests/server/forest_line.spec.luau` is NEW (round 1 and 2 shipped the pure arithmetic with no spec at all, which was my miss): crumbs, arc position round a corner, the ORDER, the two-line deal, the signed follower speed and the sow's patience. Smoke: Shotgun in the CHARACTER at 15 s and at the end, no fault in either console, 4 lines released, worst playing sounds 4 (3 boar voices plus the world's Ambience).

Karen, 2026-10-05, seven sentences (all transcribed into `PLAYTEST.md`), of which the last is the whole specification: *"so they comming we can hear them as now is / then they can stop before entering the road or walk or trot if there are spook or shoot they run and run straigh like in real life"*. Research note `docs/research/2026-10-05-sounder-movement.md` + INDEX row. **EVERY NUMBER BELOW IS A BEFORE/AFTER MEASUREMENT IN KAREN'S OWN PLACE**: a 130 s position trace at ~13 Hz of every live animal (position, facing, speed, state, sounder, slot) with three real shots through the weapon's own remote, run thirteen times across the round. **THE HEADLINE IS A TRADE-OFF I COULD NOT DISSOLVE, AND IT IS ONE WORD IN THE CONFIG.** She asked for lines AND for no circles, and as built this round delivers one at a time -- same trace, same world, same scoring: `FILE = false` gives a 33-37 stud median gap and **149** ten-second windows of 300+ degrees of turning WHILE MOVING; `FILE = true` gives a **23.3** stud gap and **417** windows; before the task it was 35.2 studs and 295 windows. A file IS a weave -- a follower steers at a point behind an animal that is itself turning, closes at `FILE_CATCHUP`, overshoots and corrects. **Shipped ON**, because she described the line twice and the circles once, and left as one word so the answer to "no, the other way" is `MOVE.FILE = false` and no code change. **WHAT IS BETTER WITHOUT QUALIFICATION.** (1) **THE CROSSING IS A WALK AGAIN**: in the road band, walk **11% -> 47%**, run **25% -> 15%**, median speed **18.0 -> 11.0 studs/s**. (2) **THE FLIGHT IS STRAIGHTER**: deviation from the heading an animal set off on, median **84-99 deg -> 34-40 deg**. The cause was that there was no heading -- `_fleeDirection` recomputed "away from the threat" from the CURRENT position every frame, so the away-vector swung round as the animal ran past the gun. `MOVE.FLIGHT` latches it once at the spook and only `steerAroundTrunk` deflects it; one animal ran exactly the 300-stud target. (3) **THE LATERAL PASS WORKS IN BOTH DIRECTIONS** (task 123 round 8's open item): L-to-R **18.9 -> 110.3 studs** across her front, R-to-L **68.0 -> 236.9**, against a span of 150. **THE DIRECTION WAS NEVER WRONG** -- the measurement is what showed that; the SPAN was, because an 11-stud beater is inside `SENSE.FLUSH_STUDS` so the herd fled SOUTH instead of walking across. 22 puts it outside the flush band and inside the notice band, so they AVOID, which is a lateral escape. (4) **THE SOUNDER SURVIVES LONG ENOUGH TO BE A GROUP**: a wave of eight became a sounder of two within about eight seconds, every member expelled by a break-away rule measured **to the leader** -- a column of eight at the file's own pitch is 52 studs long against `BREAK_STUDS` of 60, and "a member that left never rejoins". Measured **link by link** now. (5) The edge stop exists (`Brain:check`, a chance and 1-4 s; the world says WHEN and the Brain says WHAT, the same door `urge` and `spook` use). `MOVE.EDGE_*` is an **unmeasured dial** and the research note says so in its own section. **TREES: NO CHANGE. 19 distinct (boar, trunk) contact pairs before and 19 after**, and nothing stuck in either. **FOUR THINGS WERE BUILT, MEASURED, AND TAKEN OUT, with their numbers left in the code so nobody re-derives them.** A **turn budget** made it worse at every setting (4,660 -> 4,281 -> 5,326 -> 7,728 windows) because while held it re-fed the heading through `steerAroundTrunk`, which adds 55 deg every time a whisker fires: a hold that re-deflects is a rotation generator. A **speed-scaled whisker** bought 24 contacts against 24 and cost turning. Replacing the **home pull** and the **sounder centroid** -- both genuine orbit shapes -- changed nothing measurable, so both are reverted rather than kept on a hunch. **TWO OF MY OWN MISTAKES, REPORTED BECAUSE THEY COST THE ROUND.** (a) My circle metric counted turning regardless of TRAVEL, so a STANDING animal turning its head scored as an orbit -- including the ones this task's own edge stop creates -- and I tuned against that artefact for several rounds. It became visible because round 1 moved the footsteps to `rbxasset://` ENGINE assets: those load on the same frame, where a marketplace id was still loading and so not yet `IsPlaying`. **All of it was at `Volume = 0`** -- silent, never audible to Karen -- but a silent Sound still holds a voice and a count nobody can read is a measurement nobody can use. **FIXED AT THE OWNER**: the warm-up hands its list to `ContentProvider:PreloadAsync`, which fetches without playing and is the thing that exists for this, spawned because it yields. **MEASURED AFTER, 125 s with 34 boars out: worst total playing = 2, worst steps = 1** (was 88/67). `boar_shot.spec`'s pre-roll assertion is inverted to the new contract -- no sound plays silently, and the two things the pre-roll was FOR, every track built and every volume at its configured value, are still checked. (2) **`MOVE.FILE = false`** on the Director's decision: the circles were Karen's explicit complaint, the frames showed the file does not read as a line from the stand anyway, and half the turning beats a third of the gap. What differs is the part AIMED at, and the cause was measured too: the server casts the aim ray from the client's origin and then fires from the MUZZLE, **5.70 studs away**, at the aim POINT -- and between the client's raycast and the server's cast the animal has moved. Zone parts stand only 0.06-0.15 studs proud of the trunk, so a sub-stud shift re-charges head or rear to `body` (4 rows), and twice the boar had left the line entirely and the slug met Terrain. That is replication lag and aim, not attribution, so nothing was changed: a lag-compensated cast is its own task and nobody asked for one. **Round 3, item 2 -- STAGE B, the panel**: `src/client/Hud/ReportPanel.luau` is the one writer inside the `ReportPanel` frame the Hud builds and the one binder of `DrivenHunt.Report.*`; a list of hunters (kills by kind, kills, lost, points joined from `Match` on the client) and a detail view with a five-rectangle silhouette and a dot per hit at the (u, v) the record measured, fatal in the marker's kill colour. `Report.CONFIG.SILHOUETTE` is `Boar.CONFIG.ZONES` projected through `Shape.rectOf`, and `tests/server/report_silhouette.spec.luau` recomputes every number so a zone box that moves fails the harness. `Weapon.Input` gained the tagged hold (`requestHold`/`releaseHold`/`isHeld`/`holdTags`) so a click on a row cannot fire the gun, and `Hud.start` turns the stock player list off so Tab is ours. **Round 3, item 3 -- the two tick ids**, MEASURED with `PreloadAsync`: `rbxasset://sounds/switch.wav` (0.24 s) for a hit and `rbxasset://sounds/electronicpingshort.wav` (0.72 s) for a kill -- engine assets, so no upload, no moderation and no 404. **THE FRAMES FOUND THREE DEFECTS THE READBACK DID NOT** (rule 5, and this is what it is for): the panel read as see-through over a sunlit forest; the detail header sat over the silhouettes instead of its own columns; and when the panel was made opaque it came out **entirely blank** -- its labels were at the default ZIndex 1 under a panel at 10, so every child was drawing BEHIND its own background, which was also what the "see-through" look had been all along. Every child now sits above the panel's ZIndex. **Proven in a fresh Play of the Forest Test** (standing rule A): tool in the CHARACTER at 15 s and at 103 s; **zero faults in either console**; 4 waves, 28 animals; 5 slugs, 3 down; Tab -> the list with the real row, Enter -> the detail with 4 dots, Tab -> closed.
