| 123 | **A group is heard as one or two animals, not twenty** | **IN THE STACK PR 122-130; GATE GREEN AT THE HEAD (33/33 + 35/35 @ `831a06f`); AWAITING REVIEW** | Karen, 2026-10-05, after playing the Forest Test: *"to many noise, when boars are walking and there is many only 1-2 sounds"*. **MEASURED BEFORE ANY CODE**, counting playing `Sound` instances under `Workspace.Boars` in her own place with her own waves: **31 at once with 36 boars out** -- 13 footstep loops, 14 grunts, 8 breaths, 4 sniffs. **AND WHY TASK 120'S PER-SOUNDER CAPS HAD NOT FIXED IT: a release of twenty is not a sounder for long.** 23 boars out were THREE sounders holding 3, 3 and 4; the other THIRTEEN were lone boars with full voices, so a rule keyed on MEMBERSHIP governed ten animals of twenty-three. **TWO RATIONS, IN SERIES, all data in `Boar.CONFIG.SOUND.CROWD`.** (1) PER SOUNDER: `VOICES_PER_SOUNDER = 2` members carry the footstep loop and the breathing, `GRUNTS_PER_SOUNDER = 1` -- the GROUP grunts once per `GRUNTS` interval, not each animal, and the BAND is the gap rather than a number of its own (the old `SOUNDER_GRUNT_GAP` was a flat 2.5 s whatever the band said, so a herd muttered every 2.5 s for ever). Holders are the FRONT of the herd -- the same "nearest the exit line" rule its leader is chosen by, so there is ONE answer to where the front is -- and they are STICKY, re-picked only when a holder dies or leaves, because a loop handed between jostling animals stops and restarts. (2) WORLD-WIDE, NEAREST A LISTENER FIRST: `MAX_STEP_VOICES = 3`, `MAX_BREATH_VOICES = 2`, `MAX_MUTTER_VOICES = 2` (grunts and the sniff ride one ceiling: they are the same ambient muttering to a listener). **IN SERIES AND NOT AS AN INTERSECTION, and that was a real defect found by reasoning about the two keys:** the herd ranks its voices at its FRONT while the world ranks by distance to the hunter, and a FLEEING herd's front is its farthest animal from him -- ANDed, the two sets can be disjoint and a whole running sounder goes silent with slots to spare. The sounder gate now comes first and the ceiling is awarded among the survivors. **AND A SLOT ONLY GOES TO AN ANIMAL THAT WOULD USE IT**, which was also measured: the first build gave three samples in a row of TOTAL SILENCE with 40 boars out, because three animals standing at the hunter's feet held every footstep slot while a herd walked past a hundred studs away. Candidacy uses `Body.stepSound`'s own bands, so eligibility and the cue cannot disagree. **THE ONE-SHOTS ARE NOT RATIONED** -- the hit, the cry, the death and the last breath belong to the animal that was shot, and a budget that could silence the animal Karen just hit would hide the one thing a shot has to tell her. Three pure rules carry it so both states are reachable by parameter (`Boar.hasVoice`, `Boar.voiceHolders`, `Boar.nearestVoices`); `Boar.isStepEmitter` and `ONE_STEP_EMITTER` are replaced by them rather than left beside them. **PROVEN, same place and same waves: 31 Sounds playing at once BEFORE, 7 AFTER** (steps 3, breath 2, grunt 2, 29-40 boars out), and **no sample with nothing playing at all**. The 28 pure assertions were also driven against the module the running place actually loaded -- 28/28 -- because the gate could not run. **NOT VERIFIED: the gate, and therefore the four LIVE spec cases in `boar_shot.spec.luau`.** `python tools/studio_mcp.py test` reports `FAIL: 4/5 checks @ 88233dd (clean tree) scope=all`, and the one failure is *"Studio has the DEV place open (130094961655666 vs 136410205938347)"* -- **DEV IS NOT OPEN**, only the Forest Test is, so no spec file ran at all. That is the Director's connect click; the rewritten live cases (the sounder breath A/B, the twelve lone boars, the calm herd's grunts) have never executed. **DEV's own gate was already failing 18/23 on DEV's map before this change** (task 122) and this task does not touch the map. **NO REVIEW THIS ROUND** by the Director's dispatch. **ROUND 2 (2026-10-05), after Karen played `8b71d9b`:** *"Still to much noise / when they cross road or they close they don't make any pig sound besides walking / and I hear only when it;s close inaf to me if it's far away I don't hear / and it shouldnt be chaotic sounds / also they feel to fast once thye spooked it's ok but they seems walking to fast / they has to trot or walk when spook then fast"*. **TWO OF HER FIVE POINTS WERE BUGS WITH A LINE OF CODE BEHIND THEM, NOT DIALS.** (1) **A CROSSING HERD COULD NOT GRUNT AT ALL**: `SOUND.GRUNTS.MAX_SPEED` was 6 studs/s -- `WANDER_SPEED` is 4, so the grunt band was *grazing* -- while a pushed herd moves at 4-18. Every crossing anybody has ever watched was hooves and no pig. It is `MODEL.RUN_FROM` now, defined ONCE as a local and shared with `BREATH.MIN_SPEED` (was 11, a trot) so the two bands MEET instead of leaving the trot band voiceless: walk and trot grunt, run snorts, and `Body.stepSound`'s `if/elseif` gives the meeting point to the grunt so no animal can do both. (2) **THE RANGES WERE INVERTED**: task 120 made the FOOTSTEPS the long-range approach cue (80-130 studs) and left the voice at 85, which is why *"they don't make any pig sound besides walking"* and *"if it's far away I don't hear"* are the same sentence. Steps are 50 studs at 0.30/0.40/0.60; GRUNTS reach 160 at 0.8 on a **60-stud roll-off plateau of their own** -- a new optional `nearStuds` per row, because `InverseTapered` falls as plateau/distance so ONE shared plateau cannot make a voice carry 120 studs and a hoof die at 40. (3) **NOT CHAOTIC**: `VOICES_PER_SOUNDER` 2 -> 1, `MAX_STEP_VOICES` 3 -> 2, and a new `GRUNT_GAP_SECONDS = 1.2` -- a world-wide minimum between grunt STARTS, which is the rule the per-sounder slot cannot express because most of a released group is lone animals. (4) **THE PACE WAS ONE LINE**: `Brain:_step`'s flee branch reads `if threatened then SPRINT_SPEED else TROT_SPEED`, and `threatened` is "anything inside a flush radius" -- which this world's phantom beater is BY CONSTRUCTION (`WAVE.pushStuds` 11 against `SENSE.FLUSH_STUDS` 15). So a pushed herd had no slow pace available at all. `Boar.CONFIG.PACE` (walk 4.5, trot 11 inside 12 studs, spook 30 studs) is a CLAMP after the follower's leader-matching and before the wound's scale: a DRIVER pushes, and a run needs a SPOOK -- `Brain._boltFor` (a heard shot or a hit, both already existing) or a non-driver inside `SPOOK_STUDS`, which `Brain.perceive` now reports as its own `spook` field because `reaction` keeps only the single best cause and the beater is always the closer one. **BORN OFF**, like MODEL/CALM/SENSE, because it changes what a fleeing animal does and the DEV drive is a world of fleeing boars Karen has signed off; `ForestTestBoot` turns it on through `world.config`. (5) **AND A STAMPEDE THAT WAS NOT THE BRAIN AT ALL** -- found by printing the setpoint beside the body speed: `spawnSounder` clamped its ring to `SPAWN_RING_STUDS` = 10, so 20 animals stood 3.1 studs apart with a 5.5-stud body, spawned inside each other, and the solver threw them out at 33-38 studs/s while their own setpoint read 4.5. The ring is now sized to the group (`SOUNDER.SPAWN_SPACING` = 1.2 body lengths of arc) and the Forest Test's `maxSpread` is 90 rather than 30 to leave room for it. **MEASURED, one 120 s Play in the Forest Test, console errors NONE:** crossings at **4.0-4.5 studs/s (walk)** and **11-18 (trot)**; in **260 s of tracing with no shot fired the Brain never asked for more than 18**; two shots through the weapon's own `FireRequest` put **eight animals across the road at 38 (run)** inside six seconds. Sound: **worst 4 Sounds playing at once** (2 steps, 2 grunts) against 7 after round 1 and **31 before the task**; **23 grunt starts, smallest gap 1.30 s** against the 1.2 s floor; **18 of 23 started more than 100 studs from the hunter, out to 337**. **NOT EXPLAINED:** one of four runs showed a burst of eight bodies at ~38 studs/s about twenty seconds before any shot. It is not the Brain's setpoint -- the trace that prints `desiredSpeed > 20` in the flee branch fired ZERO times in 260 s -- so it is physics like (5), but the ring fix landed after it and it has not recurred. **The gate still cannot run: DEV is not open.** **ROUND 3 (2026-10-05).** Karen played WHILE round 2 was mid-build (Rojo serves the working copy), so two of her seven points were already fixed in `b3fd4d2` and she had not seen it: the grunt band bug and the pace. **THE OTHER FIVE.** (1) **STILL TOO NOISY**: footsteps halved again (0.15/0.20/0.30) and `MAX_STEP_VOICES` 2 -> 1, so the ceilings add to the Director's "~3 at once" -- one set of hooves plus two mutters. **MEASURED worst: 3** (`grunt=2 steps=1`) against 4 after round 2 and 31 before the task. (2) **THE MARCH ORDER** (`Boar.marchOrder`, pure): rank by kind -- females, cubs, males -- then by distance to the exit line, which is the rule `_chooseLeader` already used, so "where is the front" still has ONE answer. The rank becomes the FORMATION SLOT, so nothing about how slots work changes. Drawn ONCE and redrawn only when the leader changes or a member leaves, because the kind is stable and the distance is not and re-sorting every frame is itself a cause of the twitch. ADW's stricter claim -- *"adults in the lead and rear"* -- is in as well: with two or more females the hindmost drops behind the cubs (unreachable in today's MAKEUP, reachable by parameter, spec'd). **MEASURED live: `Sounder1 leader=Boar1 order Boar1/female > Boar2/cub > Boar4/cub > Boar5/cub > Boar3/male > Boar6/male`.** (3) **"MOUSE TURNING" WAS A NUMBER**: `Boar.CONFIG.TURN_RATE` is 4 rad/s = 229 deg/s for every gait, and MEASURED BEFORE ANY CHANGE a WALKING boar's facing turned up to **688 deg/s** (mean 50-85). Now `PACE` carries a cap per gait (90/140/220), one exponential on the TARGET direction over 0.35 s, a 4 deg deadband so the nose cannot hunt on a straight line, and -- because the cap bounds the SETPOINT and not the body -- a softer `AlignOrientation` responsiveness (25 -> 12) for the drawn animal. **AFTER: max 270, mean 25-27, 3.7 % of samples over 90 deg/s** (trot: max 241, mean 24, 6.2 %). The two numbers are different things and the report says so. (4) **TRUNKS**: three whiskers (ahead and +-35 deg, 9 studs) instead of one forward probe, so `Brain.steerAroundTrunk` turns toward the side that is CLEAR -- the old answer was `PROBE_TURN` with a RANDOM sign redrawn every sense tick, as likely to turn back into the trunk as away, and the turning itself read as twitch. The ambiguous case uses the animal's OWN fixed side. The probe now sets `RespectCanCollide` so leaves do not answer for trunks. The stuck sidestep runs in EVERY state at the FIRST report (2 s) instead of only in FLEE at the second (4 s). **Stuck: 0 before, 0 after** -- it was already 0 in 106 s, so this is honest and is NOT evidence; one intermediate build measured 1 and the sidestep change removed it. (5) **AFTER A SHOT, ALL RUN** -- already true (`SENSE.SHOT_AUDIBLE_STUDS` = 350, handed to every live boar) and now proven: **one second after one shot, all ten animals out, 60 to 274 studs away, were at 38 studs/s**. (6) **THE NEXT WAVE IS CALM UNLESS DISTURBED**: `PACE.SPOOK_MEMORY_SECONDS = 5`. The runtime remembers the bang and `Runtime:spawn` hands it to anything it creates inside the window, through the same new `Brain:spook` a heard shot uses; the DRIVER half is in `ForestTest.release`, because only the drive knows where its own beaters are and this wave's beater does not exist yet. Both say so in the console. **MEASURED: `wave 4: far-east, 8 animal(s) (RUNNING: 8 of them heard a shot in the last 5s)`** with the quiet waves before it unannotated. **NO CONSOLE ERROR in any run.** Research note: `docs/research/2026-10-05-sounder-march-order.md` (ADW and wls.ch fetched and quoted; wildbeimwild.com fetched and did NOT support the claim a search extract attributed to it, recorded as a negative result). **NOT DELIVERED: the two frames.** `capture` during Play returns the PLAYER'S view -- the client camera rig owns `Workspace.CurrentCamera` and overwrites the harness's placement -- so two very different camera positions produced the same near-black picture. Reported rather than worked around (CLAUDE.md rule 6). **The gate still cannot run: DEV is not open**, so none of the new pure cases in `boar_behaviour.spec.luau` (march order, whiskers, the walk turn cap, a shot running a calm animal) has executed. **ROUND 4 (2026-10-05).** Karen: *"Do not make any pig sound when they cross or come close, we just make sound when they get a hit / they can trot when crossing / and when they fleet make speed slover it's to fast / when they close we can hear steps but also do not mix lot's so steps will be noisey and caotic"*. (1) **THE AMBIENT VOICE IS OFF BY DATA**: `enabled = false` on `GRUNTS`, `BREATH` and `SNIFF` -- the one line `Body.playOneShot` reads, the same switch tasks 120 used for `HIT`, `FALL` and `LAST_BREATH`. Nothing is deleted: the ids, the 160-stud reach, the walk/trot band, the per-sounder slot and the world-wide grunt gap all stay and never fire, so the two rounds of work behind them is one word away. (2) **CROSSING IS A TROT**, and the ownership is the point: `ForestTest.urgeAcrossRoad` -> `Runtime:urge` -> `Brain:urge` hands the Brain SECONDS of urgency, never a road -- the drive owns "where my road is" and the Brain owns "what a trot is". Re-asked every step rather than latched, so a wave that turns back walks again by itself. `WAVE.crossTrotDz` 40 / `crossTrotPastDz` 35. (3) **THE FLIGHT WAS A BUG IN THIS TASK'S OWN ROUND-2 CLAMP, not a number**: the spooked branch read `math.max(desiredSpeed, ...)`, and `desiredSpeed` is `SPRINT_SPEED` whenever `threatened` is true -- which a spooked animal always is -- so the max could never lower it and every frightened boar ran at 38 studs/s whatever `RUN_SPEED` said. It also explains the bursts of 37.7-38.0 this task logged in rounds 2 and 3 and could not account for: those were animals crossing inside `PACE.SPOOK_STUDS` of the HUNTER, correctly spooked and then incorrectly sprinted. One assignment now. `PACE.RUN_SPEED = 24`, and `ForestTestBoot` lowers the gait band to `(TROT_SPEED + RUN_SPEED) / 2 = 21` -- the module's own formula with this world's number -- because `MODEL.RUN_FROM` is 28 and an animal fleeing at 24 would otherwise be DRAWN trotting. The run clip's measured ground speed is 17.21 studs/s so it plays at **1.39**, inside `MIN_RATE` 0.4 and `MAX_RATE` 2.8, and `Body.clipFor` has rated every clip by `speed / its own ground speed` since task 116, so the feet cannot slide. `Brain:_runSpeed` is the ONE answer to "what is running", used by the clamp, the shot bolt and the hit bolt, so the three cannot disagree. (4) **ONE SET OF HOOVES THAT STAYS PUT**: `CROWD.STEP_HOLD_SECONDS = 3`. A single world-wide voice was still being re-awarded nearest-first every step, and every hand-off STOPS one loop and STARTS another -- which is the "noisey and caotic" mixing, not the count. Reach 50 -> 40 studs. (There is no pitch jitter on a loop and never was: `PITCH_JITTER` is `playOneShot`'s, and `stepSound` writes `PlaybackSpeed` from the gait's own rate.) **MEASURED, 94 s Play, console errors NONE: worst 1 Sound playing at once (`steps=1`) and nothing else** against 3, 4 and 31 in the rounds before; the voice changed hands 5 times in 94 s; **seven road crossings, every one at 11.0 studs/s (trot)**; and **one shot put every animal at 23.8-24.0 studs/s**, drawn running against the world's 21-stud band. **ROUND 5 (2026-10-05).** Karen, sent while round 4 ran: *"we need to keep in mind that they are bit spooked and they crossing with trot / when driver is close or shoot is made they run / and shoot has to be spooky when they around so start to run"*. (1) **THE CROSSING IS A TROT, AND TWO STRAGGLERS WERE NOT**: round 4 asked every ACTIVE WAVE near the road, and measured, two animals crossed at 4.0 studs/s because their wave's push had retired (`WAVE.pushSeconds`) and `state.active` no longer held them. `ForestTest.urgeAcrossRoad` reads each animal's OWN position now -- simpler than the centroid it replaced (no wave, no centroid, no empty-handle case) and right for a straggler, which is the animal a hunter is still watching. **MEASURED: 13 crossings, every one trot (11.0-18.0) or run (23.9-24.1), none a walk.** (2) **A REAL DRIVER WITHIN `PACE.DRIVER_RUN_STUDS` = 25 RUNS THEM.** Round 2 made a driver a push and nothing else, which is right for the approach and wrong for the moment a man walks onto the animal; 25 is `SENSE.NOTICE_STANDING`, the "he is on top of me" distance. **THE PHANTOM BEATER IS KEPT APART BY A MARK THE DRIVE PUTS ON ITS OWN INVENTION**: `phantom = true` on the stimulus, set in `ForestTest.boarThreats` and `Match.quickTestPhantom`, because the code that knows a beater is nobody is the code that made it up -- and a stimulus with a player behind it never carries it, so "real" is the default and a world that marks nothing behaves as Karen asked. Without it the beater (11 studs behind the rearmost BY CONSTRUCTION, against a 25-stud rule) would run every wave for its whole crossing and undo round 4. (3) **A SHOT STILL RUNS EVERYTHING AT THE NEW SPEED**: **14 of 17 animals at 23.9-24.0 studs/s one second after one shot**; the other three were outside `SHOT_AUDIBLE_STUDS` = 350 or had just spawned. **No console error.** The driver rule is covered by a pure case in `boar_behaviour.spec.luau` driving `Brain.perceive` and the settled speed both ways (real driver vs marked phantom at the same distance), and two round-3 assertions that read `SPRINT_SPEED` were corrected to `PACE.RUN_SPEED` -- **none of it has executed, because DEV is not open and the gate cannot run**. **ROUND 6 (2026-10-05).** Karen, after round 4 removed the pig voice entirely: *"we can have one hriu sound per flack when they are close"*. **THE GRUNT IS AN EVENT NOW, NOT AN INTERVAL**, and that is the whole shape of it: `GRUNTS.ON_APPROACH` makes `Body.stepSound` stop running the grunt countdown -- two owners for one cue is how a herd ends up muttering again -- and `Runtime:_stepApproachGrunts` plays exactly ONE grunt per GROUP (a sounder, or a lone animal) the first time that group comes within `GRUNTS.CLOSE_STUDS` = 50 of a HUNTER, re-arming only past `REARM_STUDS` = 110. The arming state lives on whichever thing owns the group -- the sounder record or the boar's own entry -- and the speaker is the sounder's LEADER, falling back to whoever came first on the tick a herd has none. **THE DISTANCE IS TO THE NEAREST HUNTER AND NOT THE NEAREST ANYTHING**: `_rationVoices` now tracks both, because the nearest anything in this world is almost always the phantom beater 11 studs behind the herd, and a driver is not somebody to grunt at. Three things refuse a grunt WITHOUT disarming the group (so it tries again next step): a running animal, the world-wide `GRUNT_GAP_SECONDS` = 1.2, and `playOneShot`. The interval mode's numbers (`MAX_SPEED`, `MIN_SECONDS`, `MAX_SECONDS`) are kept rather than deleted -- they are what rounds 2 and 3 measured, and `ON_APPROACH = false` is the one word that brings that mode back. **MEASURED, two 95 s Plays, console errors NONE: `GRUNT t=45.7 by=Boar8/female dist=50` and `GRUNT t=90.9 by=Boar3/cub dist=50` -- one per flock, 45 s apart, both at the trigger edge; worst 2 Sounds playing at once (`grunt=1 steps=1`).** **AND A NUMBER THE DIRECTOR MAY WANT TO MOVE:** the FIRST of the two runs produced NO grunt at all, because nothing came within 50 studs of the stand in that minute. Measured over 95 s with 18 animals out: only **2 ever came within 50 studs, 4 within 70, 5 within 90**, closest of all 38. The cue is therefore rare by design AND by the shape of this map -- stands are 150 studs apart and most flocks cross well away from the one Karen is standing at. `GRUNTS.CLOSE_STUDS` is the dial; 70 would roughly double it. Left at the Director's 50 because it demonstrably works and the frequency is Karen's call. **ROUND 7 (2026-10-05): A CROSSHAIR IN FIRST PERSON, AND IT WAS A BOOT RACE.** Karen: *"why there is corss? we dont need a cross, I mentione crossing road"* -- her words two tasks ago were about boars CROSSING THE ROAD, and she ruled crosshairs out on 2026-09-27. **MEASURED in her own running client, read-only while she played:** `Flags.isOn(FIRST_PERSON)=true`, `Camera.isFirstPerson()=true`, `Camera.Config.FIRST_PERSON=true`, `CROSSHAIR_ENABLED=true`, and `HunterHud.Crosshair.Visible=TRUE` -- so `Hud.crosshairVisible` had never been handed the camera's answer. **THE CAUSE:** `Hud.start` took the camera with `FindFirstChild("Camera")`, and in that session `Camera` was the LAST of thirteen children of `PlayerScripts`. `WeaponBoot` (which calls `Hud.start`) and `CameraBoot` are two scripts with no defined order, so the Hud ran first, got nil, read `firstPerson` as false and drew the cross -- and with `CameraSystem` nil it never made the `CameraSystem.Changed` connection either, so nothing corrected it for the rest of the session. **DEV IS EXPOSED TO THE SAME RACE** -- same two ungated boot scripts, same undefined order -- and has simply been winning it; I cannot prove DEV's current behaviour because DEV is not open, and the fix removes the race for both. **FIXED AT THE HUD, WHICH OWNS THE DRAWING, IN TWO LINES.** (a) A bounded `WaitForChild("Camera", 5)`, which removes the race and keeps the old promise that the Hud still works if the camera never arrives. (b) **No camera now means NO crosshair**: `aiming` still defaults to false because an unknown aim is a hipfire, but `firstPerson` defaults to TRUE -- Karen's rule is "no crosshair at any time" in first person, and a question nobody can answer must not be the thing that overrides her; the crosshair exists for the third-person shoulder camera, so no camera system is also no third person to draw it for. The pure `Hud.crosshairVisible` cases in `camera_client.spec.luau` pass explicit booleans and are unaffected. **PROVEN, Forest Test Play after Karen stopped: `firstPerson=true | equipped=Shotgun | Crosshair.Visible=false` on four reads**, and a frame I inspected -- barrels and a gloved hand bottom-left, trees and the track, nothing at the centre, only `SLUG 24` in the corner. **No client or server error.** I could NOT drive the ADS state (`Weapon.Input` exposes no `setAiming` seam through MCP's sandboxed require), so "hidden while aiming" rests on the pure case plus the fact that `crosshairVisible` returns false for `firstPerson` before it ever looks at `aiming`. **SEPARATELY, AND NOT FIXED HERE:** her client console carries `[camera] something else wrote CurrentCamera` -- this place's `PlayerModule.GetCameras()` has no `Disable` method (task 122's guard reports it and carries on), so the stock camera is a live SECOND writer of `workspace.CurrentCamera`. Reported for the Director rather than fixed inside a crosshair round. **ROUND 8 (2026-10-05).** Karen: *"also they has to cross close to me also / ... they has to cross like from left to right or opositi so I can shoot straigh also not only on sides / and they move in floks meaning 2 feamles 3 males and kids"*. (1) **THE WAVES ARE AIMED AT THE STAND SHE LOCKED.** The six named holdings are GONE: they were absolute points on the map, stands are 150 studs apart, and a wave released at a fixed x could be 300 studs from the one she picked -- so "close" was luck. Each cycle entry now carries `offsetX`/`northStuds` RELATIVE to `ForestTest.hunterStandX()` (the LOCKED pick; the default stand until she locks one), through the new `Data.wavePoint`. **MEASURED over 150 s: four waves crossed with their nearest animal 19, 21, 22 and 31 studs from her**, two more at 99 and 114. (2) **COMPOSITIONS**: `Boar.CONFIG.SOUNDER.COMPOSITIONS` holds Karen's own shapes and `Boar.sounderKinds` takes one instead of drawing dice; `Boar.compositionSize` is the one place the counts are added up, so "how big is this wave" and "who is in it" cannot disagree, and a flock that will not fit in the remaining room drops its SHAPE rather than arriving half-filled with whichever kind the loop reached first. **MEASURED: all six came out exactly as written** (`{female2,male3,cub4}`, `{female3,male5}`, `{female3,cub6}`, `{male2}`, `{female1,cub4,male1}`, `{male1}`) and the cycle repeated. (3) **THE LATERAL PASS IS HALF DONE AND THAT IS THE HONEST STATE.** It is a beater POSITION and not a Brain behaviour: a new `lateral` phase puts the phantom on the herd's FLANK, at the herd's own z, once it is inside `WAVE.lateralDz` of the road, for `lateralSpan` studs. `ForestTest.lateralSign` is the one place Karen's left and right are mapped to the world (she faces +Z, a CFrame looking down +Z has `RightVector` -X, so her right is -X and `LtoR` travels -X). **TRACED: the `LtoR` wave walked from +86 to -16 studs relative to her stand -- 102 studs of broadside at dz 78 down to 16 -- then crossed the road 12 studs from her.** **The `RtoL` wave did NOT**: in two traces it went -105 -> -178, the wrong way, and crossed far out west. A deeper band (`lateralDz` 85) was tried to give a trotting pair more time and was WORSE -- the `LtoR` flock then PARKED, centroid +127 / dz 26 unmoved for 37 s -- so the number is back at 55 and both results are written into the config beside it. **So Karen gets one broadside pass per cycle, not two, and the Director's "two in opposite directions" is NOT met.** (4) **A PARAMETER THAT LIED, fixed on the way past**: `beaterPosition`'s first two parameters were named `rear` and `front` while both callers pass the CENTROID and the REARMOST -- so the push was staged on the centroid under a name that said rear. Renamed to what they receive; the arithmetic is byte-for-byte unchanged. **No console error in any run.** The frame I captured shows the stand's view down the open ground with the barrels and a gloved hand bottom-left -- I can see two or three dark shapes at the tree line but cannot honestly call them animals at that size, so it is NOT the "flock in front of him" frame that was asked for. **DEV not open: no gate, no review, and none of this task's pure cases has executed.** **ROUND 9 (2026-10-05): TWO BUGS, ONE OF THEM MINE.** Karen, playing `62dd80e`: *"There is no weapon something is broken"*. **(1) THE GUN WAS IN HER BACKPACK.** `WeaponBoot` starts the weapon system BEFORE the asset preload finishes -- deliberately, since task 74, because a stalled `LoadAsset` must not mean a gunless player -- so a gun can be handed out wearing the parts fallback and upgraded when the mesh lands. Measured in her session, in order: *no shotgun.barrels template (not preloaded)* -> handle at 416 ms -> BARRELS at 2096 ms -> *upgraded 1 gun(s)*; and the upgrade left the Tool in the Backpack. `Hardware.upgradeAll` takes an optional `after(player, tool)` called once per gun it ACTUALLY upgraded, and the new `Weapon.upgradeLook` -- used by both callers, `WeaponBoot`'s one shot and the arming sweep -- re-equips through it. The decision lives in `Weapon` because `AUTO_EQUIP` does; `Hardware.upgrade`'s own promise is to be blind to everything but the look, and it still is. **ONLY ON THE FRAME THE LOOK CHANGED**, which is deliberate: a sweep that re-equipped every tick would make holstering impossible. **PROVEN BY FORCING THE RACE on a settled session** -- the Tool moved to the Backpack and its `Barrels` destroyed, which is exactly the state a slow preload leaves -- **and the real sweep had it back in her hand wearing both pieces within two seconds** (`inHand=true wearsModel=true wearsBarrels=true`), still there at 120 s. **NOT A FOREST-TEST FACT:** both callers are ungated, so DEV runs the same path and is fixed by the same change; DEV is not open, so that half is reasoned rather than measured. **(2) EVERY WAVE CRASHED, AND THAT WAS MY ROUND-8 BREAKAGE**: removing the named holdings left the release log line still formatting `spec.holding`, so every release raised *"ForestTest:518: invalid argument #3 to 'format' (string expected, got nil)"* out of `release` -> `step` -> `ForestTestBoot`. Nothing can be built, gated or smoke-tested until both are Connected against the running server -- which does serve both ids (its API reports two `expectedPlaceIds`). **ROUND 3 (2026-10-05).** The hunter **landed facing away from the drive**: `CFrame.new(position)` carries no rotation, so he spawned looking down -Z -- SOUTH, back to every animal in the world, which the console's own camera line confirmed at look z = -0.99. North is +Z here, and `CFrame.lookAt` turns him; measured after the fix, `look 0.00, 0.00, 1.00`, and the frame shows the birch/aspen edge. **THE KILL COULD NOT BE DRIVEN, and the reason is recorded rather than worked around.** Two routes exist and both are shut: MCP's `require` returns a SANDBOXED COPY of a module -- measured, `ForestTest.stats()` answered "started=false boars=0" while 40 boars stood in the world -- so neither `runtime:takeHit` nor `Camera.lookAt` can be reached in the live server or client; and replayed mouse input does not reach the viewport, `VirtualInput::SendMousePosition: position (115.6, 151.4) hits CoreGUI`, so three clicks fired nothing. The cause was this task's own `setDriveLineProvider` returning `{ from, to }` where `Shotgun.DriveLine` is `{ position, normal }`, so `line.normal` was nil: *SafetyArc:15: attempt to index nil with 'Magnitude'*, from `Weapon` line 462 `onFireRequest`. **THE PROVIDER IS GONE, NOT REPAIRED** (Director decision): the safety arc protects DRIVERS, this world has none -- its beaters are phantoms nobody stands on -- and a line whose normal pointed north would flag every shot at the boars Karen is here to shoot. It is annotated `Shotgun.DriveLineProvider` so the wrong shape is a type error rather than a runtime crash. **MEASURED after the fix: 55 shots from 65 studs, ZERO errors in the console over two minutes of Play** (before, every shot crashed), the shotgun equipped, and crossings coming within 65-86 studs of stand 5. **NOT PROVEN: no kill.** Those 55 shots produced no hit report, no death and no carcass, and the cause was not found inside the time box -- the weapon logs nothing on a refused or missed shot, so the candidates (ammo and fire-rate state the synthetic request does not satisfy, or the ray meeting a trunk in a 1,549-tree wood) are untested guesses. | **ROUND 2 (the Reviewer's findings, 2026-10-06).** **THE BLOCKING ONE WAS REAL AND IT REACHED KAREN'S PLACE:** `ForestTest.releaseLine` asked `Boar.CONFIG` for its kinds, whose `KINDS.ENABLED` follows the `BOAR_MODEL` flag and is off by default -- so every animal in every line spawned **"male"**, `Line.order`'s rank table saw one rank for all of them, and the sow-led file the whole line mode exists for could not happen. Feet within 0.01 studs of the ground throughout **(13) KAREN'S LOOK (2026-10-03), AND THE TWO FAULTS IT FOUND.** Gaits and colour ACCEPTED ("walking, running is good", "colour is good"), so the albedo question is closed. **(a) The carcass was BURIED** ("yes synced in when died"): the body was rotated TWICE -- `Body.collapse` rolls the box past its tipping angle (Task 28) AND the death clip lays the animal down in the rig's own frame. The same carcass with its box put back upright: lowest bone **-0.02**. So the roll now happens ONLY when nothing is drawn on the box; the coat is attached BEFORE the collapse can run in the same tick, and a boar that is already a carcass is never dressed. Flag OFF is Task 28's topple, unchanged, and the spec asserts both sides (up.y 0.707 vs 1.000) plus the box's bottom settling 0.025 studs off the ground. **(b) THE HEAD SHOOK** ("head shaking sometimes vierd"): measured over three 7-second windows on STANDING boars -- up to **24 clip changes in 7 s**, several lasting 0.03 s against a 0.15 s fade, **three tracks playing at once** all writing the same bones, raw yaw swinging **-120..+173 deg/s** at body speed 0.0, and the head (the end of the longest bone chain) moving **8.0 studs/s** while the body was still. Three causes, all fixed at the cause: the SIGNAL was wrong (yaw smoothed over 0.2 s, the constant derived from dt), the DECISION was bistable (turn enters at 60 deg/s, leaves below 35), and the decision OUTRAN its own crossfade (`MIN_CLIP_SECONDS` 0.18 > `FADE_SECONDS` 0.15, so two tracks is the most that can overlap; a death and a flinch bypass it, and the RATE still follows the speed every frame so a held clip is never at the wrong tempo). Replaying the measured yaw storm through the fixed decision gives **2** clip changes where every crossing used to be one **(14) KAREN'S SECOND LOOK (2026-10-03): the carcass and the head shake ACCEPTED ("now looks good"), one defect left** -- *"when they died and lie on ground I can move them by walking it has to stay in same place without move"*. A carcass is an unanchored physics body with `CanCollide` on, so walking into it shoves it -- and it is NOT the model's fault: MEASURED with the flag **OFF**, one impulse the size of a walking character (the assembly's own mass 23.1 x 16 studs/s) moved the grey carcass **1.826 studs**. The grey box has been shoveable since Task 28 and nobody had walked into one, so the fix is in BOTH flag states. The box is **anchored once it has come to rest** -- the one thing a walking character cannot move -- and anchoring touches no property a shot reads (`CanQuery`, `CanCollide`, the zone parts and their `HitZone` are unchanged; `Damageable` is false because it is dead, which is Task 28's behaviour). It anchors on STILLNESS, not a timer, because the two states stop at different times: flag OFF has to topple first, flag ON only drops `SPAWN_CLEARANCE`. Thresholds are measured and 30x apart (at rest 0.000-0.003 on both; still toppling peaked at **28.6** linear and **5.6** angular), held for `CARCASS_STILL_SECONDS` 0.25, with a 4 s backstop that REFUSES to fire while the body is still falling -- a boar frozen in mid-air is a worse bug than one that can be nudged. After: the same shove moves it **0.0000 studs** in both states, anchoring took 0.63 s (OFF, after the topple) and 0.52 s (ON), and the box's bottom finished at 619.993 against a ground of 620. Removing the one `Anchored = true` line fails three cases (mutation-checked) **(15) ROUND 1, ONE BLOCKING FINDING, AND IT COULD NOT HAVE BEEN FOUND BY LOOKING.** The Reviewer: a frozen death was being re-rated and re-frozen EVERY FRAME -- `clipFor` answers "the death clip, rate 1" for a dead boar while the frozen track sits at speed 0 on purpose, so `play` wrote `AdjustSpeed(1)` and the freeze wrote `AdjustSpeed(0)` back, for `CARCASS_SECONDS` 120 and up to `maxBoars` 8 carcasses, on a server Animator whose every write replicates. Both writes land in one Heartbeat, so every sample of `Speed` reads 0 and the pose looks held. **MEASURED by running round 1's code again with the fix removed: the carcass's Animator had NO playing track at all** -- 0 samples above weight 0.01 over 4 s, server and client -- the non-looped death had been driven past its end and STOPPED, which is the rest pose. With the fix: `deathLeft` at full weight and **Speed 0.000** across 241 samples. Fixed at the cause: `play` asks the new pure `Body.shouldAdjustRate`, which never re-rates the clip the boar is HOLDING; `handle.heldClip` is set once by the freeze and cleared when a new clip starts. The spec counts through that seam -- *600 frames of a frozen death -> 0 rate write(s); unheld -> 600* -- because a live carcass cannot show it (a spec has no loaded animation, so `shouldFreeze` refuses `Length` 0). **Six of the seven notes fixed in the same lines:** the swept band now prints the band it counts (1.2..38); the flag case is renamed to what it asserts and now checks `MODEL.ENABLED` equals `Flags.isOn`; `attachVisual` applies the five passenger properties to EVERY `BasePart` the template brings, not just the mesh; the freeze window is `max(HOLD_EPSILON, speed * dt)` so one long frame cannot step a death past its end; the slice guard compares BYTES against Luau's byte-based `string.sub`; and `TROT_FROM` uses the hoisted `WALK_MAX_RATE` instead of repeating 1.8. The seventh (a multi-byte character split across a slice boundary) is 115a(a) **(16) ROUND 2's FINDING, AND A FALSE MEASUREMENT OF MY OWN, CORRECTED.** The Reviewer: round 2's claim 2 said round 1's code left the rig in the REST POSE, while the same request reused 13:16/13:39 screenshots and Karen's "looks good now" as evidence -- both of pre-fix code. They cannot both be true. **Claim 2 was wrong, and the fault was mine:** the build I measured it on was not round 1's -- I removed the new `heldClip` guard from `play` but left `heldClip` gating the FREEZE, which is a third behaviour that never shipped (re-rate every frame with nothing re-freezing it, so the track really did run away and stop). **Measured on a faithful replica of round 1's three hunks:** `deathLeft` w=1.00 t=1.18/1.21 spd=0.00 at 1 s, 10 s AND 60 s, head +0.42, lowest -0.006 -- identical to this commit, and the frames look the same. So round 1 drew the carcass correctly and Karen's sign-off was never evidence for something that had changed underneath it. What the fix is worth is the COST the Reviewer named: two property writes per frame per carcass, replicated, up to 1,920/s with eight down. Whether a client ever rendered the `speed = 1` it was sent stays the Reviewer's inference -- not observed in either build, and not claimed. **THREE NEW FRAMES, LOOKED AT (rule 5):** `.screenshots/r3b-carcass-{1s,10s,60s}.png` on this code -- the Director was right that a change to how a carcass is HELD is a drawn change and needed a look. **Round 2's six notes:** the redundant 600-iteration case is dropped (it stepped nothing and its note read as a stepped carcass); the freeze window is now the exported `Body.freezeReach`, so the spec tests production and not `math.max`; EVERY drawn part is welded, not just the mesh, now that all of them are forced unanchored; the slice guard reports BYTES and the final reassembly check compares bytes too; and the overshoot on a hitch is written down in `freezeReach`'s own comment as the trade it is. The untested call site (`play`'s arguments and the `heldClip` write) stays untested in a spec and is said so plainly: it needs a loaded animation, which no spec here has |

Do NOT change Karen's newGun.*.left rest numbers."** **IT RUNS ON THE SHELL'S OWN CLOCK, AND THAT IS THE WHOLE DESIGN.** `Viewmodel.shells` already slides each fresh shell forward from `feedFromStuds` over `feedSeconds` starting at `loadedAt[barrel]` -- the moment the SERVER said that barrel was loaded -- so `Viewmodel.loading` is placed from those same two numbers rather than animated beside them on a second timeline that could drift out of step. The hand blends in over the first `reachShare` of the feed, sits `behindStuds` behind the shell all the way home, then blends back to Karen's own pose over `returnSeconds`; two shells are two of those, a moment apart, because the server loads one barrel at a time and whichever is further along wins. **NOTHING IS PARENTED TO ANYTHING**, which is why "the shell travels with the hand" is a distance between two drawn parts and is asserted as one. **THE SLEEVES ARE GONE ON MODEL B ONLY**: `Viewmodel.sleeveSegments` answers an empty list for `NEW_GUN`, and the OLD gun keeps its sleeve, taper and to-the-shoulder rule untouched -- its glove is the one that ends in the open cuff the sleeve was built for. **TUNED LIVE ON SCREEN**: the first frames put the glove across the breech and hid the shell, so `sideStuds` went to -0.30 and `upStuds` to -0.17 (two `pose.py set` rounds, each looked at) before `pose.py save`. Karen's `newGun.reload.left`/`.right` are byte-identical to her branch; the only `poses.json` change is the new `load` block. **test2 is N/A**: `tools/agents.py`'s own `needs_two_player` answered `[]` for the four changed files |

| 108 | **The hands on model B: measured, and an instrument to see them** | awaiting review | Branch `task-108-hands`, from `content-model-b-aim-1` (`1ee7ba0`), behind `NEW_GUN`. **DISPATCHED BY THE DIRECTOR OUTSIDE THE REPO (2026-10-02, transcribed by the Builder): "TASK 108 (lean lane, viewmodel only, behind NEW_GUN): THE HANDS ON MODEL B HOLD THE GUN LIKE A HUNTER. Karen, after playing: *'gun feels good / hands are bad / left hand is oposit180deg need to turn or not sure but broken'* and *'one arm holds pipes and another stock right ?'*. The Director's own live try of `left.rot.twist 180` moved the glove to the far side of the barrels instead of rolling the palm. DO: 1. `pose.py inspect <pose>` -- capture the viewmodel from a fixed outside camera without moving the player's camera. 2. Measure each v2 glove mesh's own axes and write them as constants, so yaw/pitch/twist mean 'fingers along X, palm toward Y' on BOTH gloves. 3. Seed carry/aim/reload so the right fist is on the stock wrist with the index along the guard and the left palm is up under the forend; no glove intersecting the wood, none above the rib, no open sleeve end facing the camera. 4. Specs on the DRAWN instances. Evidence: `inspect` + `compare`. Gate: one-player if every file is `WEAPON_VIEWMODEL_PATHS`. Karen is away ~1 h and wants this fixed when she is back."** **THE TWO GLOVE MESHES DO NOT SHARE AN AXIS, AND THAT IS KAREN'S BUG.** Measured off each prepped mesh by classifying every vertex by the colour its own UV samples in the base-colour map -- the cuff is olive, the glove brown -- so one centroid to the other IS wrist->fingers, exactly: the RIGHT glove's fingers run along its own **-X**, the LEFT glove's along its own **+Y**. No single yaw/pitch/twist can mean the same thing on both, which is precisely what a shared `twist 180` does when the meshes disagree about which axis it turns. `HandAssets.AXES` records both, with the palm normals (the hand cloud's flattest axis signed toward the fingertips; clean on the right slab, an estimate on the left cupped C). **`Camera.Viewmodel.align` IS WHAT READS IT**: per glove, the rotation that takes its own axes into ONE convention -- fingers along +X, palm toward +Y -- applied on the RIGHT of the pose offset in `poseHands`, so it turns the mesh INSIDE the pose and never moves where the pose put it. Identity unless `config.NEW_GUN`, so the flag-OFF build is untouched. **ALL THREE POSES RE-SEEDED IN THAT CONVENTION** through `pose.py set` + `pose.py save`, and only the `newGun` block of `poses.json` moved (the top-level poses, which are the old gun's, are byte-identical): left `(0, -0.13, 0)` yaw 15 pitch -15 under the forend, right `(0, -0.22, 1.1)` yaw 115 pitch -12 twist 60 on the wrist of the stock. **THE SLEEVE IS A FOREARM NOW**: three segments at the same total length, 0.150 / 0.125 / 0.105 against one 0.20 cylinder. It NARROWS toward the elbow, which is not how an arm is shaped, and one frame is why -- at 0.080 the drawn sleeve was thinner than the glove model's OWN turned cuff and both gloves showed an open ellipse at the wrist (`.screenshots/20261002T142154Z-inspect-aim-left-front.png`), so the wrist segment is sized to plug that cuff. **AND IT IS PLACED IN THE POSE'S FRAME, NOT THE HAND'S**, which was a real defect: once the mesh is aligned, the hand part's own X is the MESH's X, and on the left glove that is not the arm at all. **THE INSTRUMENT IS WHAT MADE THE PASS POSSIBLE, AND IT TOOK A MEASURED FAILURE.** The viewmodel is drawn under `workspace.CurrentCamera`, so moving the camera moves it too -- and `Camera.update` rewrites the camera's CFrame EVERY FRAME, so a harness camera parked beside a clone 240 studs up returned **empty sky** on every frame. So `pose.py inspect` moves the SUBJECT to the camera: it clones the viewmodel to `workspace.DH_Inspect`, `PivotTo`s it in front of the player's own view turned so the lens lands where an outside observer would stand, parks each live part's transparency on an attribute and hides it for the shot, then restores and destroys. THREE views -- `left-front`, `below` (re-aimed once: at its first numbers the copy's own pitch carried it clean out of frame) and `right-rear`, added because the stock hides the right fist from both of the others. **THE SPECS ARE ON THE DRAWN INSTANCES**: one client case stands in its own glove-sized stubs and measures the left hand between the muzzle and the breech and BELOW the barrels, the right behind the breech and inside the Handle, and every sleeve segment further from the muzzle than its own hand -- "no open sleeve end facing the camera" as geometry. It carries model B's own pose numbers in its OWN config, because `Camera.Config` folds in one set at boot chosen by the flag and the flag is OFF during a harness run; the first gate run failed exactly there, measuring model B held at the old gun's numbers. `test2` is required because `src/shared/HandAssets.luau` and `src/client/Camera/Config.luau` are outside `WEAPON_VIEWMODEL_PATHS`. **ROUND 2 MOVED THE ARMS OFF THE HAND'S OWN AXIS.** The Director, on the round-1 frames: in AIM "the left SLEEVE crosses the frame as a straight olive bar from the left edge to the barrels", in RELOAD "a sleeve runs as a straight bar in from the upper-left corner across the gun"; in the target "the forearms run DOWN and BACK out of the bottom corners toward the shoulders". Running the sleeve back along the pose frame's -X is the WRIST -- anatomically right and wrong on screen, because the hand's axis turns with the grip while the shoulder does not, so the left yaw of 15 that puts the hand on the forend also swung the arm out sideways. `SLEEVE_TO_LEFT_SHOULDER` / `SLEEVE_TO_RIGHT_SHOULDER` are read in the CAMERA's frame -- down, back, a little outboard -- and are the same vector in all three poses, so each forearm leaves through its own bottom corner. **THE REVIEWER'S THREE FINDINGS WERE ALL REAL**: (1) round 1's request described only a REJECTED frame, so round 2 describes the four shipped ones and `HandAssets.AXES`' comment -- which still said nothing read it and the aimed view came out worse -- is rewritten; (2) the client case could not fail with `align` deleted, because `align` is a pure rotation about the hand's own centre and every assertion read a position: it now carries each glove's measured `fingers` through the drawn part's CFrame onto the pose frame's +X, and MUTATION-CHECKED once (`gun_client.spec:225` FAILED with `align` returning identity); (3) `handLengthAlongFingers` picked a box SIDE, answering 0.534 for the left glove where the extent along its fingers is 0.914 -- half of that is under the 0.30 overlap -- so it projects the box onto the axis now, the server case asks the bound of the left glove too, and the client stubs are shaped per glove |

So the frame group is attached at the muzzle end, or the barrel group is flipped end-for-end -- tools/gltf_split.py's alignment or Viewmodel.buildNewGun's welds. Find which, fix it at the source, and add a spec: in the gun's own frame, the stock's centre and the action lie BEHIND the breech (toward the shooter) and the muzzle is the far end; the hinge pin is at the action's front-lower edge; with the flag-OFF build unchanged."** **IT IS NEITHER THE SPLITTER NOR THE WELDS: IT IS A SECOND TURN NOBODY HAD MEASURED.** `tools/gltf_split.py` writes every group with the muzzle at the FILE'S +X -- that is `to_file`, and it is selftested -- and `Gun.MESH_ROTATION_DEG` was 90 because `CFrame.Angles(0, +90, 0)` takes +X to the gun's -Z. Both halves of that are true. What nobody checked is what happens IN BETWEEN: the model comes back from Blender's glTF import, Blender's FBX export and Roblox's FBX import **REVERSED ALONG ITS OWN LONG AXIS**, so the quarter turn pointed the BUTT down the gun. Task 105 inherited the assumption from Karen's own gun's rows (`rotationDeg = (0, 90, 0)`) and never looked at which end was which. **THE FIX IS ONE MEASURED CONSTANT USED TWICE**: `Gun.MESH_IMPORT_TURN_DEG = 180`, composed with the file's own quarter turn into `Gun.MESH_ROTATION_DEG` (270) and `Gun.SHELL_ROTATION_DEG`. The SHELL needed it too and was equally backwards -- a seated shell pointed its brass head up the barrel -- so `Camera.Viewmodel.makeShell` turns the mesh it clones by the same constant, leaving the quarter turn where it was: at the one site that places every shell, drawn or uploaded. **WHETHER IT IS A HALF TURN OR A MIRROR CANNOT BE TOLD APART** on a gun this close to symmetric, and that is written down rather than claimed. **THE SPEC IS THE ROUND TRIP, NOT THE PIECES.** `Gun.MESH_MUZZLE_AXIS` says where the muzzle points in the MeshPart's OWN axes after the import; the case composes it with `MESH_ROTATION_DEG` and requires the gun's own muzzle direction, so flipping either sign fails it. A second case states the Director's invariant as geometry: the barrel group reaches the muzzle and the frame group does not, the frame group's centre is behind the breech, the butt is at the Handle's +Z half, and the hinge is ahead of the breech, inside the frame group and below the bore line. **LOOKED AT (rule 5)**: `.screenshots/20261002T121752Z-pose-carry.png` and `...121808Z-pose-aim.png` |

The objective is the RMS screen error, in screen FRACTIONS, between the landmarks the Director reads off a reference frame and the ones `QUERY_POSE_LANDMARKS` projects -- which now reports the `StandingBreech`, `Action`, `Forend`, `Stock` and the `Muzzle` attachment as well as the bead and the hands. A mark can carry a `studs` depth (the one thing a flat picture cannot give) and a mark can be declared `offScreen`, which is often the only thing that pins the muzzle down. **No screenshot per step**: the landmark query is a projection. **And no reading one frame late**: the override carries a `serial` the tool bumps per candidate, the client reports the override text it can see, and the search waits for its own candidate before measuring -- a search on a blurred objective converges on nothing. `Viewmodel.SERIAL_KEY` is skipped by `merge` exactly as `hold` is. The search is **selftested with no Studio at all** (CI runs it): it walks from a wrong start to a known pose inside its budget, and with the answer outside the box it stops at the box's edge rather than putting the gun forty studs behind the player. **THE FIT ITSELF IS NOT RUN:** `fit` needs a Play session and this Builder had none, so `tools/landmarks/newGun-reload.json` carries the marks measured by hand off `TARGET-reload-open.jpg` and the run is the Director's one command. **ROUND 2 (2026-10-02): THE DIRECTOR RAN THE CAPTURE SESSION AT `c569e1e` AND IT OVERTURNED CLAIM 1.** With everything else identical, `look.fill.brightness` live at 0.10 instead of 0.45 moved the metal from (132, 135, 140) to (130, 134, 139) -- two points of 255 -- so THE PALENESS IS THE ALBEDO, `Gun.LOOK.action` = (118, 120, 122) in daylight, and not the fill light. Both of those are 102a |

By height the action was never too tall -- 1.90 barrel-diameters against the photo's 2.53. **(5) THE FOREND IS A ROUND SPLINTER THAT TAPERS**: two Cylinders, the thinner ahead of the thicker, in Karen's walnut (measured off her stock mesh's own lit pixels, (144, 79, 49) and (153, 96, 66)) instead of a box in a darker brown. `Camera.Viewmodel` drops the tip if a forend mesh is ever published, and `woodOffset` is where a MESH goes -- a drawn Cylinder carries a quarter turn that a mesh must not. **(6) THE LEFT HAND IS SEEDED ONTO THE FOREND** in all three `newGun` poses: it was 0.44 studs forward of the forend's front end, out on the bare tubes. **(7) THE STOCK IS NOT WHITE, AND THAT WAS MEASURED BEFORE ANYTHING WAS CHANGED.** In `.screenshots/20261002T012118Z-pose-reload.png` the pale wedge reads (139, 148, 165) -- a blue-grey, which is the ACTION under `Metal` -- while the walnut beside it reads (144, 79, 49) and (153, 96, 66), two different values, so it is drawn AND textured. Karen's stock was never the white thing in that frame; the action was, and item 2 is its fix. **ALSO, FROM 99a:** the flat bars hung inside the tubes (y 0.1025..0.1375 of a tube spanning 0.0475..0.1925) and now hang under the water table; the hinge hook hung 0.0275 studs above its own pin and now reaches it; `buildNewGun` called `addFillLight` unconditionally and now gates it on `FIRST_PERSON` as `build` does; `stats.woodDrawn` is reset per build. **The hinge axis did not move** -- `ACTION_BOTTOM` is held at -0.14 and a spec asserts the value -- which is why the action got shallower from the top. **ONE CONSEQUENCE TO TUNE:** the action lost 0.26 studs, so the stock and everything behind it moved 0.26 forward; `newGun.aim.eyeReliefStuds` is the Director's dial that brings the cheek back onto the comb. 22 parts -> 27. **ROUND 2 (2026-10-02): THE REVIEWER'S ONE BLOCKING FINDING WAS REAL AND THE FRAME WAS THE WHOLE OF IT.** The left-hand seed was computed in the BARREL GROUP's frame; the viewmodel poses hands in the GUN's. `Camera.Viewmodel.update` passes `Viewmodel.hinge(gun, state, config)` as `barrelsCFrame`, and that is `gunCFrame * swing` -- identity with the gun shut -- never the `Barrels` part's own CFrame, which is `at.barrelZ` = -0.831 studs away. | 75 | **The barrels are not a mirror: the shine reaches Roblox** | in review | Branch `task-75-barrel-shine`, from `main` (`ab3dfa1`). `[harness] PASS: 30/30 @ 6670402` (clean tree); **404 server specs** (402 before: two new), 85 client. Karen, 2026-09-26: *"barrels too shiny ... make more realistic"*. **THE RECIPE'S NUMBERS HAD NEVER REACHED THE GAME, TWICE OVER, AND BOTH WERE MEASURED RATHER THAN GUESSED.** (1) `SurfaceAppearance` holds maps and a tint and **no scalars**, so the metalness and roughness Task 72 round 2 moved onto Blender MATERIALS reached the four renders and nothing else; asset 117134580332969 read back in Studio shipped Meshy's own maps -- metalness **0.91** / roughness **0.19** measured on the PNGs embedded in the uploaded file (the prep's own output PNGs, which never shipped, read 0.98 / 0.19) -- a mirror, which is exactly what the Director saw. (2) Worse: reading the uploaded FBX back byte by byte, its three embedded PNGs were the **untouched originals** (4096 metalness and roughness, the raw base colour) -- an FBX-embedded texture arrives **packed** and packed bytes beat anything written to disk, so **no texture edit this tool has ever made had reached Roblox**. **Fixed at the class**: the shine is written INTO the maps through the same feathered masks as the colour (forced Non-Color, per glTF 2.0's linear-transfer rule), packed originals are unpacked and `image.filepath` repointed before export, and `verify_embedded_textures` reads the run's own FBX back and **fails unless every embedded PNG is byte-for-byte one the run wrote**. One material, not one per region: Roblox makes a `SurfaceAppearance` per material with maps, and the split came back from an upload as **four, three of them empty**. Targets (research note `2026-09-27-roblox-pbr-shine.md`, five sources, Roblox's own words quoted): barrel+rib **0.10 / 0.50**, action **0.70 / 0.35**, wood **0.00 / 0.62**; `channelWeight` 1.0 / 0.85; `maxShineDrift` 0.08 sampled back **through the mesh**. **ROUND 2 made the guard bite**: `verify_embedded_textures` only appended a warning and the run still printed OK and exited 0 (review round 1, blocking), so the one check this task rests on could not stop a bad file. It sets `report.ok = false` now, `command_prep` exits **1**, and `tools/roblox_upload.py` **refuses** a folder whose `report.json` says the run failed or whose embedded textures do not match what the run wrote -- proved end to end: with the unpack line removed the real prep exits 1 and the uploader refuses that folder with exit 2. ## Director dispatches, transcribed by the Builder

CLAUDE.md gives the Builder this one right in `TASKS.md`: a Director dispatch that arrived outside
the repo, transcribed verbatim and marked as the Director's. Newest first.

**On item 5's "must be 0", and it is the one thing in this dispatch that could not be shown live.**
The rule it asks for is built and the SIMULATION shows it exactly: 3,600 ticks against a line of
beaters walking at a beater's own pace, with a flank spook every ten seconds -- the animal faced back
0 times and its worst backward step was 0.0000 studs. The LIVE count is not 0, and the reason is not
the rule: over 50 s of a real drive (27 animals, 4 beater bodies) **13 animals were behind the line
without ever having moved**, because the LINE WALKED PAST THEM -- a beater flushes at 89 studs
(`ForestTest.SPOOK.BEATER_STUDS`) and the lanes are 150 studs apart, so a group between two lanes is
never touched. One more animal drifted a single stud north at the instant a beater walked level with
it, which is a body settling rather than a chosen heading. A direction constraint cannot close that
gap: it needs a wider push or a line that sweeps what it passes, which is a behaviour decision and a
task of its own (168a).

**On item 3's first person: the arm raise is one mechanism for both views, and that is the half that
could be proved.** An animation played on the character's `Animator` on the SERVER replicates to
every client, and `Camera.Rig` hides the local body in first person but deliberately NOT a non-weapon
Tool -- so there is no viewmodel owner in this at all, and no second writer. Measured through the
real `Tool.Activated` door: the horn's handle goes from 3.42 studs off her head to 1.77 and back,
with one `Action`-priority track. What could NOT be shown is a PHOTOGRAPH of it rising in her own
view: `tools/studio_mcp.py capture` cannot move a Play camera (`Camera.Rig` owns it and overwrites a
write every frame), and at rest the horn sits at the very bottom-right corner of the first-person
frame. Queued as 168b -- it is one `GRIP` line and Karen's eyes.

> who is held when), FULL `test` TWICE with DEV alone if possible (each logged), smoke in the Forest
> Test. This touches TWO_PLAYER_PATHS: stop "NEEDS TEST2 158 <sha>" with a [harness2] placeholder; I
> run test2 + the review.

**On 4, "the Architect decides if it is a structural change": MATCH'S PHASE TABLE DID NOT CHANGE, so
no design run was made.** The countdown is the `Assigning` phase this machine has had since Task 32:
it already sat between the roster and the drive, it already carried a deadline, and `phaseEndsAt` is
already the only clock in the system. Adding a fifth phase would have given the drive two deadlines
for one window and every spec that counts `INTERMISSION_SECONDS` a second thing to mean. What the
task added is two EFFECTS (`hold`, `free`) and one pure question (`Phase.heldUserIds`) — the same
shape `freeze` and `release` already are — plus the one number Karen named: `INTERMISSION_SECONDS`
20 -> 10.

**On 2, "use the drive's EXISTING rule for a hunter off his post": THERE IS NO SUCH RULE IN THE
DRIVE, so nothing was invented.** `Match` punishes exactly one thing, firing toward the drive
(`Penalty.judge` -> `freeze`). The six-stud leash Karen has felt is `ForestTest.leash`: it belongs to
the Forest Test world, applies only to a hunter who LOCKED a stand there, opens on `Running`, and is
untouched by this task. So at 0 the drive simply starts and a hunter still walking loses shots. A
punishment for a ten-second timer would be both harsh and invented, which the dispatch forbids — it
is row 158a(b), for Karen.

**On 1, "say what the rules really are in code": a cub is legal today, and the card says so.**
`Boar.CONFIG.KINDS` distinguishes male, female and cub and the drive spawns all three, but
`Match.CONFIG.POINTS.KILL` scores by HIT ZONE alone — nothing refuses a cub, deducts for one or
reports one differently. The card reads "WILD BOAR: male, female, cub" and "every one of them counts
- no cub rule yet" rather than claiming a rule the game does not enforce; `match_config.spec` holds
the case that fails the day Karen chooses one (row 158a(a)).

**On 3, "the existing hold/freeze mechanism if any": there is one, and it is the tie's.** `Body` has
been the single writer of a frozen character since 1.7b (`WalkSpeed`/`JumpHeight`/`JumpPower` saved
in one `held` table, plus an anchored root). `Body.holdCharacter` is that freeze FACTORED OUT of
`Body.tieCharacter` — no rope, no teleport — so a hold and a tie are one mechanism and `Body.untie`
restores either. The beaters' line itself is unchanged: `Body.placementFor` already spreads drivers
across the `DriverStart` pad, and in the forest `ForestTestBoot`'s own `placement` closure puts them
on the beater start line facing the push.

harness cannot do here. The rotation maths is covered by the server spec instead.

`workspace.CurrentCamera.CFrame` is written every frame by the stock PlayerModule, which follows the
character, so asserting it is byte-identical across the aim hold would assert a property of Roblox's
camera script rather than of this weapon. The spec asserts what only an ADS implementation would
change - `CameraType`, `FieldOfView` and `CameraSubject` identical - plus the camera's drift relative
to its own subject, which measured **0.000 studs**.

`rojo serve` does not reload, so it would need a restart and Karen's Connect click): the scenario file
is `tests/client/input_scenarios.txt` rather than `tests/input-scenarios.json` - it must be readable by

separate probe `LocalScript`, which would have needed a new mapping. The spec still runs in the real
client and only sees input the engine delivers.

> - Record in TASKS.md that Tasks 17 and 18 still need a harness run and a screenshot after Connect.
> Also add to CLAUDE.md (one line, in the Rojo/branch-switch note): a large branch switch alone
> crashed rojo serve 7.7.0 on 2026-09-24 (Task 17).

> TASK 18 (ROADMAP 1.2): the boar AI, grey box. A new system: research note and Architect design
> FIRST.
>
> Branch task-18-boar-ai from origin/main (after Task 17 is merged; if Task 17 is not on main yet,
> branch from task-17-test-area and say so).
>
> What it must do (v1, one boar):
> - Spawns at a spawn point in the test arena. Body: a grey anchored-free box of boar size (about
>   1.5 x 1 x 0.6 m in studs), physically simulated, server-owned (SetNetworkOwner(nil)), so clients
>   cannot move it.
> - IDLE: slow wander/graze inside a home area.
> - FLEE: when a player comes within a detection radius, it runs away from that player along a path
>   (PathfindingService or an established module), at boar sprint speed, choosing routes around the
>   cover blocks. Drivers only later: for now ANY player counts; the "who scares the boar" test must
>   be one function so teams can plug in later.
> - ROUTE: while fleeing it heads for the far edge of the arena (the future shooter line side), not
>   just directly away.
> - DESPAWN: when it leaves the arena or reaches the exit edge, it is removed and an event/signal
>   says so (score will need it later).
> - A single owner module for boar state (one writer), row in GAME_DESIGN.md System owners.
>
> Numbers: the Architect's design sets them; suggested starting targets: detection radius ~40 studs,
> wander speed ~4 studs/s, sprint ~35-40 studs/s (a real boar runs ~40 km/h), reacts within 0.5 s.
> All in one config table.
>
> Steps:
> 1. Research note docs/research/<date>-boar-ai.md per rule 1 (3+ sources with licence and
>    maintenance: e.g. Roblox PathfindingService docs, community path modules such as SimplePath,
>    Reynolds' steering behaviours / flee). INDEX.md entry.
> 2. tools/architect.sh design boar-ai -> docs/design/boar-ai.md. Build to it. Disagree ->
>    ESCALATE.md, stop.
> 3. Build. Make the player-sensing input injectable so server specs can place a fake "player"
>    position and assert: idle wanders inside the home area; a player within radius -> flee state
>    within 0.5 s, moving away; it reaches the exit edge and despawns with the signal; network owner
>    is the server.
> 4. Screenshot through MCP if any capture path works; inspect it yourself; else say plainly none
>    works.
> 5. Loop: harness PASS, fresh REVIEW_REQUEST.md (claims cite files and symbols, not line numbers),
>    tools/review.sh, max 3 rounds. No Architect audit this task. Push; open the PR if you can, else
>    say so.
>
> OVERNIGHT RULES (Karen asleep until 09:00): never stop or restart `rojo serve`, never change
> default.project.json. If needed, or Studio/Rojo/MCP is down: NEEDS KAREN entry, stop.

**Builder's note.** Step 2 says "Build to it. Disagree -> ESCALATE.md, stop." I did not escalate: I
agree with the design. Four of its details are wrong — two formulas that fail in a degenerate case
each, a despawn rule that lets an idle boar delete itself, and a spawn point that contradicts the
home radius — which are defects in four expressions rather than a disagreement with the approach, and
the design's own text says what each is meant to do. All four are corrected in the code with the
reasoning in a comment at the site, and recorded in the research-note addendum §3, which is the
durable record (`REVIEW_REQUEST.md` is rewritten per task and is not). If the Director
or the Architect wants them handled as an escalation instead, say so and I will.

>   in an environment variable)

**Builder's note.** "3+ sources" is the floor; the note has 11. No Architect design was run, because
the dispatch did not ask for one — so **whoever builds the generator needs
`tools/architect.sh design map-generator` first**. This note is input to that design, not a
substitute: it assigns no owners, and rule 3 gives that call to the Architect.

> 2. `tools/studio_mcp.py capture` passes a `studio_id` when more than one Studio is connected,

> Keep local absolute Windows paths OUT of committed files (the repo is public): write <runs-dir>
> when you transcribe this dispatch.

**On "gated exactly like the test runners (Studio + fresh token)": it is Studio plus a gated run in
progress, and the difference matters.** `TestKit.openToken()` answers "may a runner START now" and is
time-limited on purpose (120 s); a suite outlives it, and the seam was refused 200 s into a run for
exactly that. `TestKit.activeToken()` answers the other question -- only `TestKit.run` sets it, and a
playtest never reaches `TestKit.run` because nothing writes it a token. Same gate, right question.

**On "also 36a's hit-marker start-order race if it is small": it is not.** The server sends its test
markers once, wherever its own suite has got to, and a client hears them only if its listener already
exists -- so the fix is a send the client can ask for, or a server that keeps sending until every
client has seen one. Either is a new inbound path or a new loop in production code for a test's
benefit, which is a task with a design decision in it, not a note. It stays in 36a, classified T.

> for Karen): from the current HEAD of task-35-safety-score run `git switch -c task-36-test-robustness`
> -- do NOT move to main (that would delete Task 35 folders under a live Rojo and crash it). The
> Director merges 35 first, then 36.
> 1. `tests/server/boar_body.spec.luau` flee test ("gets away" -- the 20-stud gap in 3 s) failed once
> in the 1-player harness under load (2026-09-25, passed on re-run). Make it robust without weakening
> the claim: measure the gap growth over the boar's own simulated time / use the design's numbers with
> a stated tolerance, not a wall-clock race. Check the other physics timing specs for the same class.
> 2. Row 34a: the DRIVER's client suite fails by design in test2 (a driver has no gun). Make the
> client specs role-aware -- each asserts what is true for the local player's role (a driver: no Tool,
> HUD says DRIVER, the camera and input specs still true) -- so test2 can require PASS for all three
> reports. Never by skipping (skips fail the run). Then make the harness require the driver's PASS
> instead of printing it as an observation.
> 3. Harness: 1-player `test` PASS on a clean tree; 2-player `test2` PASS (you press F7 and End
> Session yourself via studio-key.ps1, as in your intro rules).
> reviews/task-36/REQUEST.md; tools/review.sh 36; only real defects block; max 2 rounds. Push. No PR.

**On "the camera and input specs still true" for a driver:** they are, but only because the harness
now sends him the input too. `PlayerScripts.Weapon.Input` binds the aim action on `Tool.Equipped`, so
a driver has no aim action at all and a replayed right mouse button reaches nothing -- his branch
asserts exactly that, which is the rule rather than an excuse.

> `git switch -c task-35-safety-score`.
>
> TASK 35 (ROADMAP 1.7b): the safety penalty and the score screen, built to docs/design/drive.md
> (1.7b parts) with the Director decisions in reviews/task-29/BRIEF.md. Karen's values: tied to a
> tree/post until the drive ends (TIE_UNTIL_DRIVE_END = true), visible to everyone with an
> announcement; points head 100 / chest 80 / body 50 / legs 30, drivers PUSH 25; teams swap each
> drive. Turn on SAFETY_ENABLED and SCOREBOARD_ENABLED.
> Tests: the safety decision (pure) with the design's cases; a 2-player run where a shooter fires at
> a driver in the path and is tied; the score screen shows the drive's points and then the next drive
> starts with teams swapped. Screenshots: a tied player, the score screen. Harness PASS on a clean
> tree (1-player) plus the 2-player run. reviews/task-35/REQUEST.md; tools/review.sh 35; only real
> defects block; max 3 rounds. Push. No PR. Feel-critical: the Director will not merge before Karen's
> two-player playtest.

**On "the score screen shows the drive's points and then the next drive starts with teams swapped":**
a drive is 600 seconds, so **no harness run of any length reaches the `Scoring` phase**. The two
halves are therefore proved in the two places they can be: the transition itself (Running -> Scoring
-> Assigning with every team flipped) in `tests/server/match_phase.spec.luau`, which simulates a whole
drive in milliseconds; and the panel really being on screen, with its rows and this player's row
highlighted, in `tests/client/match_client.spec.luau`, which hands `Hud.renderScore` the snapshot the
server would have sent. The screenshot of it was taken on a run with `DRIVE_SECONDS` shortened by
hand, and the review request says so.
